\displaystyle\prec\E(1-r_{1})\underbrace{\E(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}^{\top})}_{=I_{K+2}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\E\{(1-r_{1})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\}^{\top}\E\{(1-r_{1})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\lVert\E\{(1-r_{1})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\}\E\{(1-r_{1})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\}^{\top}\rVert_{2}<\E(1-r_{1})\lVert\E(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{1}^{\top})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\E(1-r_{1}),

which implies
\hat{\sigma}^{2}=m^{2}\{c+o_{P}(1)\}
for some constant
c>0
. Lastly,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{2}=

\displaystyle\tilde{n}^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}P_{[\bm{1},\bm{C}]}^{\perp}\bm{y}_{I}-\{\tilde{n}^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}\bm{x}-\tilde{n}^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]\hat{\bm{M}}^{-1}(n^{-1}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]^{\top}\bm{x})\}\hat{v}^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\{n^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}\bm{x}-n^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]\hat{\bm{M}}^{-1}(n^{-1}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]^{\top}\bm{x})\}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{M}}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]^{\top}[\bm{1},\bm{C}],\quad\hat{v}=n^{-1}\bm{x}^{\top}P_{[\bm{1},\bm{C}]}^{\perp}\bm{x}.

Since
\bm{x}
is mean 0 and independent of
\{\bm{y},\bm{C}\}
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}\bm{x}-n^{-1}\bm{y}_{I}^{\top}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]\hat{\bm{M}}^{-1}(n^{-1}[\bm{1},\bm{C}]^{\top}\bm{x})=o_{P}(1),

which completes the proof.
∎

S8
Theoretical guarantees for factor analysis and differential abundance

Section
S8
proves theoretical statements from Sections
5.2
and
5.3
in the main text. We prove Theorem
5.4
from Section
5.4
in Section
S9
.

S8.1
Problem statement and preliminaries

We consider the model
\bm{y}_{g}=\bm{X}\bm{\beta}_{g}+\bm{C}\bm{\ell}_{g}+\bm{e}_{g}
, where
\bm{e}_{g}\sim(0,\sigma_{g}^{2}I_{n})
is sub-Gaussian with independent entries. We also define the diagonal matrix of weights
\bm{W}_{g}=\diag(w_{g1},\ldots,w_{gn})
to be
r_{gi}\{\pi_{g}(y_{gi})\}^{-1}
for
\pi_{g}(y_{gi})=\Psi\{\alpha_{g}(y_{gi}-\delta_{g})\}
. Note that
\E(\bm{W}_{g}\mid\bm{y}_{g},\bm{X},\bm{C})=I_{n}
. Let
\bm{L}=[\bm{\ell}_{1}\cdots\bm{\ell}_{p}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times K}
,
\lambda=np^{-1}\Tr(\bm{L}^{\top}\bm{L})
, and
\bm{B}=[\bm{\beta}_{1}\cdots\bm{\beta}_{p}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d}
. If
\hat{w}_{gi}=w_{gi}
, the optimization problem in (
4.2
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle(P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}},\hat{\bm{L}},\hat{\bm{B}})\in\argmin_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{C}_{\perp},\bm{L},\bm{B}\\
\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{C}_{\perp}=\bm{0}\\
\bm{C}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{C}_{\perp}=I_{K}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2\lambda p}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{C}_{\perp}\bm{\ell}_{g}-\bm{X}\bm{\beta}_{g})^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{C}_{\perp}\bm{\ell}_{g}-\bm{X}\bm{\beta}_{g}).

Define
\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}=\bm{W}_{g}-\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}
. Solving for
\bm{B}
and using the fact that
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}=(\bm{C}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{C}_{\perp})^{-1}\bm{C}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{y}_{g}
, the profile likelihood for
\bm{C}_{\perp}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}\in\argmax_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}\\
\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{U}=\bm{0}\\
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{U}=I_{K}\end{subarray}}f(\bm{U}),\quad f(\bm{U})=\frac{1}{2\lambda p}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}

(S8.1)

Expanding
\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}
, the objective function can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} f(\bm{U})=&\frac{1}{2\lambda p}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}\\
&+\frac{1}{2\lambda p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}\\
\tilde{\bm{C}}=&P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}(\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{-1/2},\quad\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}=(\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g}.\end{aligned}

(S8.2)

We use this expression to prove the consistency of
P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}
in Section
S8.3
and derive its properties and rate of convergence in Section
S8.4
.

S8.2
Assumptions

We first re-state the assumptions on
y_{gi}
and
\Psi
from Section
5.1
with a change in the scaling of the eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{K}
defined in Assumption
5.1
. Note that the change is without loss of generality.

Assumption
S8.4
.

For
g\in[p]
,
i\in[n]
, and
s\in[S]
, let
y_{gi}=\bm{\beta}_{g}^{\top}\bm{x}_{i}+\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}\bm{c}_{i}+e_{gi}
and define
G_{si}\in\mathbb{R}
to be a random variable. Then the following hold for constant
a>0
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/2\wedge a)
:

(a)

\bm{X}=[\bm{x}_{1}\cdots\bm{x}_{n}]^{\top}
and
\{\bm{\beta}_{g}\}_{g\in[p]}
are non-random and satisfy
\lvert\bm{X}_{ij}\rvert,\lVert\bm{\beta}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq a
,
\bm{1}_{n}\in\im(\bm{X})
, and
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\succeq\epsilon I_{d}
.

(b)

The matrix
\bm{G}=[G_{si}]\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times n}
is mean 0, has independent and uniformly bounded entries, and identically distributed columns.

(c)

The eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{K}>0
of
np^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}
and
\bm{\ell}_{g}
satisfy
n^{1/2+\epsilon}\allowbreak\lesssim\allowbreak\lambda_{K}\allowbreak\leq\allowbreak\cdots\leq\allowbreak\lambda_{1}\allowbreak\lesssim n
,
\lambda_{1}/\lambda_{K}\leq a
, and
\left\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\right\rVert_{2}\leq a(\lambda_{1}/n)^{1/2}
.

(d)

\bm{C}=[\bm{c}_{1}\cdots\bm{c}_{n}]^{\top}=\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}+\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}
for
\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times K}
and
\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}_{i*}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
such that:

(i)

\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}
is non-random and
\lVert\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\rVert_{1}\leq a
,
\lVert\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\rVert_{\infty}=o(n^{-1/4})
,
\sum_{s=1}^{S}1\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{s*}\neq 0\}\leq ap^{1/2}
.

(ii)

The rows of
\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\E\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}
are independent and identically distributed,
\V\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}_{i*}\}\succeq\epsilon I_{K}
, and
\E\{\lvert\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}_{ik}\rvert^{m}\}\leq a_{m}
for all
i\in[n]
,
k\in[K]
,
m\geq 1
and constant
a_{m}>0
that may depend on
m
.

(e)

\bm{E}=[e_{gi}]=\{\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
, where
\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times p}
,
\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
satisfy:

(i)

\sup_{s\in[S];g\in[p]}\lvert\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sg}\rvert\leq an^{-1/4}
and
\sup_{g\in[p]}\sum_{s=1}^{S}1\{\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sg}\neq 0\}\leq a
.

(ii)

The columns of
\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}
can be partitioned into disjoint sets containing
\leq a
metabolites, where
\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sg}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sh}=0
if columns
g
and
h
lie in different sets.

(iii)

\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}\sim MN_{p\times n}\{0,\diag(\sigma_{1}^{2},\ldots,\sigma_{p}^{2}),I_{n}\}
, where
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{p}^{2}\in[\epsilon,a]
.

(f)

\bm{G}
,
\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}
, and
\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}
are independent, and
p,n,S
satisfy
p\in[\epsilon n,an]
.

(g)

In differential abundance applications,
\bm{X}
can be written as
\bm{X}=(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})
for
\bm{X}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{1}}
and
\bm{X}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{2}}
, where
\bm{X}_{1}
are the
d_{1}
covariates of interests and
p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}1\{\bm{\beta}_{g_{j}}\neq 0\}=o(\lambda_{1}^{1/2}n^{-1})
for all
j\in[d_{1}]
.

(h)

Assumption
5.2
from the main text holds.

Assumption
(a)
contains all the regularity conditions on the design matrix
\bm{X}
mentioned in Section
5.1
. The assumption that
\bm{1}_{n}\in\im(\bm{X})
makes the assumption that
\E(\bm{G})=0
in
(b)
without loss of generality. Note
\E\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}
in
(d)
may depend on
\bm{X}
. The assumptions in
(d)
are more general than (b) from Assumption
5.1
in the main text, since the latter assumes
\E(\bm{c}_{i})-\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}i}
is continuous in
\bm{x}_{i}
, whereas the former only assumes
\lVert\E(\bm{c}_{i})-\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}i}\rVert_{2}
is bounded from above. The eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{K}
in
(c)
have been scaled by a factor of
n
to make notation in the below theoretical statements simpler, and to be consistent with
[
35
,
52
,
51
]
. Assumption
(g)
gives the sparsity assumption utilized in the statements of Theorem
5.2
and Theorem
5.3
in the main text. Note this is only needed in differential abundance applications, and is not needed to prove Theorem
5.1
, Propoposition
5.2
, or Theorem
5.4
. We next prove two useful lemmas about
\bm{C}
and its relationship with
\bm{E}
that we will use in the theoretical results that follow.

Lemma
S8.1
.

Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds. Then
\lim_{n\to\infty}\Prob(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C}\succeq\epsilon I_{K})=1
and
\E(\lvert\bm{C}_{ik}\rvert^{m})\leq\tilde{a}_{m}
for all
m\geq 1
and some constant
\tilde{a}_{m}>0
that may depend on
m
.

Proof.

Define
\bm{\mu}=\E\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C}\succeq

\displaystyle\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}+\bm{\mu}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}+[\bm{\mu}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}]^{\top}

\displaystyle+\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}^{\top}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}+[\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}^{\top}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}]^{\top}.

First,
\bm{G}_{i}^{\top}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
by
(b)
and
(d)
in Assumption
S8.4
, meaning
\lVert P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}^{\top}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq nc^{2}
. This means
\lVert\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}^{\top}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{1/2})
. Since
\lVert\bm{\mu}\rVert_{2}^{2}=O(n)
by
(d)
in Assumption
S8.4
, we also have
\lVert\bm{\mu}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{1/2})
. Since the rows of
\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}
are independent and identically distributed,
n^{-1}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}=\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}^{\top}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}-\bm{\mu}\}\succeq\{c+o_{P}(1)\}I_{K}
for some constant
c>0
, which proves
\lim_{n\to\infty}\Prob(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C}\succeq\epsilon I_{K})=1
.

Let
\lVert z\rVert_{m}=\{\E(\lvert x\rvert^{m})\}^{1/m}
for any random variable
z
. Then for some constant
c>0
and
a_{m}
as defined in Assumption
S8.4
,
\lVert\bm{C}_{ik}\rVert_{m}\leq\lVert\bm{G}_{*i}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{*k}^{(c)}\rVert_{m}+\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{ik}^{(c)}\rVert_{m}\leq c+a_{m}
.
∎

Lemma
S8.2
.

Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, let
\epsilon>0
be any constant and
m>0
any integer, and let
h_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{K}\to\mathbb{R}
,
i\in[n]
, be uniformly bounded functions with uniformly bounded gradients. Then for
\bm{L}=[\bm{\ell}_{1}^{\top}\cdots\bm{\ell}_{p}^{\top}]^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\E\{(n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{*k}^{\top}\bm{E}_{g*})^{2m}\}\leq c_{m},\quad k\in[K];g\in[p]

(S8.3a)

\displaystyle\textstyle\E[\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1^{n}}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{E}_{gi}\}^{2m}]\leq c_{m},\quad g\in[p]

(S8.3b)

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{E}^{\top}\bm{L}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)

(S8.3c)

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{E}_{gi}\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)

(S8.3d)

for some constant
c_{m}>0
that may depend on
m
.

Proof.

Under Assumption
S8.4
,

\displaystyle\bm{C}_{*k}^{\top}\bm{E}_{g*}=\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{*k}\}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{*g}+\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}_{*k}\}^{\top}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{*g}+\bm{C}_{*k}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{*g}

Let
\epsilon>0
be any constant. Since
\bm{C}
is independent of
\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{*g}
,
\E\{(\bm{C}_{*k}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{*g})^{2m}\}\leq c_{1,m}
for some constant
c_{1,m}>0
by Corollary
S8.1
. Further, since at most finitely many entries of
\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{*g}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
are non-zero,
\bm{G}
is independent of
\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}
, and the rows of
\bm{G}
are mean 0, independent and sub-Gaussian,
\E[\{n^{-1/2}\{\bm{\Delta}_{*k}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{*g}\}^{2m}]\leq c_{2,m}
for some constant
c_{2,m}>0
by Corollary
S8.1
. Let
\mathcal{I}_{g}=\{s\in[S]:\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sg}\neq 0\}
and
\mathcal{C}=\{s\in[S]:\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{sk}\neq 0\}
. Then

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{*k}\}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{*g}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{sk}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(e)}_{sg}(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})+n^{-1/2}\sum_{r\in\mathcal{I}_{g}^{c}\cap\mathcal{C}}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{rk}\bm{G}_{r*}^{\top}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\bm{\gamma}_{sg}^{(e)}\bm{G}_{s*}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}=n^{-1/4}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)},\quad\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(e)}=n^{-1/4}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}.

First, since
\E\{(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})^{2m}\}\leq c_{3,m}
for all
s\in[S]
and some constant
c_{3,m}>0
that may depend on
m
,

\displaystyle\E[\textstyle\{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{sk}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(e)}_{sg}(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})\}^{2m}]\leq ac_{3,m}

for some constant
a>0
because
\lvert\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{sk}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(e)}_{sg}\rvert
and
\lvert\mathcal{I}_{g}\rvert
are uniformly bounded from above. Let
\bm{Z}_{g}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{sg}\bm{G}_{s*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
\bm{Z}_{g}
is mean 0, has independent entries that are bounded above by
an^{-1/4}
for some constant
a>0
, and is independent of
\bm{W}_{g}=\sum_{r\in\mathcal{I}_{g}^{c}\cap\mathcal{C}}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{rk}\bm{G}_{r*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Here,
\bm{W}_{g}
is also mean 0, has independent entries. Further, since
\{\bm{G}_{r*}\}_{r\in\mathcal{I}_{g}^{c}\cap\mathcal{C}}
are independent and sub-Gaussian with uniformly bounded sub-Gaussian norm and
\sum_{r\in[S]}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{rk}\}^{2}\leq\sum_{r\in[S]}\lvert\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{rk}\rvert\leq a
for some constant
a>0
by Assumption
S8.4
,
\bm{W}_{g}
is also sub-Gaussian with uniformly bounded sub-Gaussian norm over
g\in[p]
. All this implies

\displaystyle\E[\textstyle\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{r\in\mathcal{I}_{g}^{c}\cap\mathcal{C}}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{rk}\bm{G}_{r*}^{\top}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\bm{\gamma}_{sg}^{(e)}\bm{G}_{s*}\}^{2m}]=\E\{(n^{-1/2}\bm{W}_{g}^{\top}\bm{Z}_{g})^{2m}\}\leq c_{4,m},\quad g\in[p]

for some constant
c_{4,m}>0
that may depend on
m
. This completes the proof of (
S8.3a
).

Since
h
is bounded from above, the above work implies we need only show that
\E[\{n^{-1/2}\allowbreak\sum_{i=1}^{n}\allowbreak h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{*i}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{*g}^{(e)}\}^{2m}]\allowbreak\leq\allowbreak c_{m}
to prove (
S8.3b
). For simplicity, we assume that
\mathcal{I}_{g}=\{s_{g}\}
, and note that the extension to general
\mathcal{I}_{g}
under the Assumption
S8.4
is trivial. Then for
\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}=\bm{C}_{i*}-\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}
,

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{*i}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{*g}^{(e)}=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\{\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\{\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+O(1),

where the second equality follows because
\{h_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
have uniformly bounded gradients,
\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}
is bounded, and
\lvert\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\rvert\lVert\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\rVert_{2}=o(n^{-1/2})
. An application of Lemma
S8.4
then proves the result, which proves (
S8.3b
).

For (
S8.3c
),

\displaystyle(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{E}^{\top}\bm{L}=

\displaystyle(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\bm{C}^{\top}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}\}^{\top}\bm{L}+(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\bm{L}

\displaystyle+(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\bm{L}.

It is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\bm{C}^{\top}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}\}^{\top}\bm{L}\rVert_{2},\,\lVert(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\bm{L}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1).

For the remaining term, let
\tilde{\bm{L}}=n^{3/4}\lambda_{K}^{-1/2}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\bm{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times K}
. Then at most
O(p)
rows of
\tilde{\bm{L}}
are non-zero and
\lVert\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}\rVert_{2}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
by Assumption
S8.4
. Then for
\mathcal{S}=\{s\in[S]:\bm{\gamma}_{s*},\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}\neq 0\}
and
\mathcal{R}=\{(r,s)\in[S]\times[S]:r\neq s,\bm{\gamma}_{s*}\odot\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}\neq 0\}
(where
\odot
is the Hadamard product),

\displaystyle(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\{\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}\bm{L}=

\displaystyle p^{-1/2}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{s*}\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}^{\top}(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{(r,s)\in\mathcal{R}}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{s*}\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}^{\top}(p^{-1/2}\bm{G}_{r*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*}).

Since
\lVert\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{s*}\rVert_{2},\lVert\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}\rVert_{2}\leq c
and
\E(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})\leq c
for some constant
c>0
and all
s\in[S]
,

\displaystyle\E\{\lVert p^{-1/2}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}^{(c)}_{s*}\tilde{\bm{L}}_{s*}^{\top}(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})\rVert_{2}\}\leq c^{3}p^{-1/2}\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert=O(1)

since
\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert=O(p^{1/2})
by Assumption
S8.4
. Lastly, since
\E(\bm{G}_{r*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*}\bm{G}_{r^{\prime}*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s^{\prime}*})=0
for all
(r,s)\neq(r^{\prime},s^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\E[\{n^{-1}\sum_{(r,s)\in\mathcal{R}}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{sk_{1}}\tilde{\bm{L}}_{sk_{2}}^{\top}(p^{-1/2}\bm{G}_{r*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s*})\}^{2}]=O(n^{-2}\lvert\mathcal{R}\rvert)=O(n^{-2}p^{3/2})=O(1),

which completes the proof of (
S8.3c
).

Lastly, to prove (
S8.3d
), we again assume for simplicity that
\mathcal{I}_{g}=\{s_{g}\}
, but note that extending it to general
\mathcal{I}_{g}
is trivial under Assumption
S8.4
. Since
h_{i}
is bounded, the proof of (
S8.3c
) implies we need only consider the behavior of
\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{\ell}_{g}
. Fix a
k\in[K]
and let
a_{gk}=n^{1/2}\lambda_{K}^{-1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g_{k}}
, where
\lvert a_{gi}\rvert
is uniformly bounded from above by Assumption
S8.4
. Let
\mathcal{C}=\{g\in[p]:\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\neq 0\}
. Then
\lvert\mathcal{C}\rvert=O(p^{1/2})
by Assumption
S8.4
and for any
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{\ell}_{g_{k}}=(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{\ell}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle+(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}^{c}}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{\ell}_{g_{k}}.

Since the
\bm{G}_{s_{g}}
is independent of
\bm{C}
for
g\in\mathcal{C}^{c}
, the second term after the equality in the above expression is
O_{P}(1)
because
h_{i}
is uniformly bounded from above. For the first term, fix a
g\in\mathcal{C}
and let
\bm{C}_{i*}=\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(e)}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+\bm{R}_{i*}^{(g)}
, where
\bm{R}_{i*}^{(g)}
is independent of
\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}
. Then for
\tilde{\ell}_{gk}=n^{1/2}\lambda_{K}^{-1/2}\bm{\ell}_{gk}
(which is uniformly bounded by Assumption
S8.4
),

\displaystyle(\lambda_{K}p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\bm{C}_{i*})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{\ell}_{g_{k}}=p^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}}\tilde{\ell}_{gk}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}[n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\{\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}]

\displaystyle+p^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}}[n^{1/2}\tilde{\ell}_{gk}\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}\{\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\}^{\top}][n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}^{2}\smallint_{0}^{1}\nabla h_{i}\{\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}+t\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}dt],

Since
h_{i}
and
\nabla h_{i}
are bounded,

\displaystyle\E(\textstyle[n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\{\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}]^{2})\leq c

\displaystyle\E[\lVert n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}^{2}\smallint_{0}^{1}\nabla h_{i}\{\bm{R}^{(g)}_{i*}+t\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}*}^{(c)}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}dt\rVert_{2}]\leq c

for some constant
c>0
. Since
\lvert\mathcal{C}\rvert=O(p^{1/2})
, this completes the proof.
∎

Remark
S8.7
.

The proof of Lemma
S8.2
can easily be extended to show that (
S8.3
) holds when we replace
\bm{C}
with
P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C}
. This will be useful in Lemma
S8.17
below.

S8.3
Consistency of
P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}

Here we use
f(\bm{U})
from (
S8.2
) to prove the consistency of
P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}
. The main results are Lemmas
S8.8
,
S8.12
,
S8.13
and Corollary
S8.2
. For ease of notation, we re-define
P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}
to be
\hat{\bm{C}}
in Sections
S8.3
and
S8.4
.

Lemma
S8.3
.

Let
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}
be a matrix with orthonormal columns that satisfies
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{0}
, and define
\delta=\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{2}
. Then there exist
\bm{v}_{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
,
\bm{z}_{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-K-d)\times K}
, and some universal constant
c>1
such that

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{z}_{u}=I_{K},\quad\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u},\quad\left\lVert\bm{v}_{u}-\bm{v}\right\rVert_{2}\in[c^{-1}\delta^{2},c\delta^{2}],\quad\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}\in[c^{-1}\delta,c\delta],

where
\bm{v}=\bm{A}_{u}\bm{B}_{u}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
for
\bm{A}_{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
and
\bm{B}_{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
the left and right singular vectors of
\bm{v}_{u}
.

Proof.

We can express
P_{\bm{U}}=\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}
and
P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}
, and can always express
\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}
where
\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{z}_{u}=I_{K}
by the Fredholm alternative. Then

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq K\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=2\Tr(I_{K}-\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u})=2\Tr(\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{z}_{u})=2\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle K^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

The first two lines imply
\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\in[\delta^{2}/2,K\delta^{2}/2]
, which taken with the third line, implies
\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\in[\delta^{2}/(2K),K\delta^{2}/2]
. Note that since
\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}\preceq I_{K}
, the singular values of
\bm{v}_{u}
satisfy
0\leq\sigma_{K}\leq\cdots\leq\sigma_{1}\leq 1
and

\displaystyle 1-\sigma_{K}^{2}=\left\lVert I_{K}-\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\in[\delta^{2}/(2K),K\delta^{2}/2]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow 1-\sigma_{1},\ldots,1-\sigma_{K}\in[\delta^{2}/(4K),K\delta^{2}/2].

If
\bm{v}_{u}=\bm{A}\diag(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{K})\bm{B}^{\top}
is the singular value decomposition of
\bm{v}_{u}
, this shows that
\left\lVert\bm{A}\bm{B}^{\top}-\bm{v}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}\in[\delta^{2}/(4K),K\delta^{2}/2]
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.4
.

Suppose the random variables
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}
satisfy the following for some integer
m\geq 1
and constant
c>0
:

1.

\E(z_{i}^{2m})<c

(ii)

There exists a
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}
such that
\E(z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F})=0
for all
i\in[n]
and
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}
are independent conditional on
\mathcal{F}
.

Then
\E\{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i})^{2m}\}\leq cc_{m}n^{m}
, where
c_{m}
is a constant that only depends on
m
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\E\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\right)^{2m}\right\}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]}\E(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{2m}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\\
\text{$\exists j\in[2m]$ such that}\\
i_{j}\notin\{i_{s}\}_{s\in[2m]\setminus\{j\}}\end{subarray}}\E(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{2m}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\\
\text{ for all $j\in[2m]$, there exists }\\
\text{$j^{\prime}\neq j$ such that $i_{j}=i_{j^{\prime}}$}\end{subarray}}\E(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{2m}}),

where

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\\
\text{$\exists j\in[2m]$ such that}\\
i_{j}\notin\{i_{s}\}_{s\in[2m]\setminus\{j\}}\end{subarray}}\E(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{2m}}\mid\mathcal{F})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\\
\text{$\exists j\in[2m]$ such that}\\
i_{j}\notin\{i_{s}\}_{s\in[2m]\setminus\{j\}}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\E(z_{i_{j}}\mid\mathcal{F})}_{=0}\E\left(\prod_{s\in[2m]\setminus\{j\}}z_{i_{s}}\mid\mathcal{F}\right)=0

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\\
\text{ for all $j\in[2m]$, there exists }\\
\text{$j^{\prime}\neq j$ such that $i_{j}=i_{j^{\prime}}$}\end{subarray}}\E(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{2m}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\lvert\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2m}\in[n]:\text{ for all $j\in[2m]$, there exists $j^{\prime}\neq j$ such that $i_{j}=i_{j^{\prime}}$}\}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cc_{m}n^{m}

for some constant
c_{m}>0
that only depends on
m
.
∎

Corollary
S8.1
.

Let
r>0
be an integer, and define the
r
possibly dependent sets of random variables
\{z_{j1},\ldots,z_{jn}\}
to be such that
z_{j1},\ldots,z_{jn}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
S8.4
for each
j\in[r]
. Then for
S_{j}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{ji}
,
\tilde{S}_{j}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{ji}
, and any
t>0
,
\Prob(\max_{j\in[r]}\lvert S_{j}\rvert\geq t)\leq cc_{m}r/(nt^{2})^{m}
and
\Prob(\max_{j\in[r]}\lvert\tilde{S}_{j}\rvert\geq t)\leq cc_{m}r/t^{2m}
for
c,c_{m}
defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.4
.

Proof.

Since
\max_{j\in[r]}\lvert S_{j}\rvert\leq\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}S_{j}^{2m}\right)^{1/(2m)}
and
\max_{j\in[r]}\lvert\tilde{S}_{j}\rvert\leq\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}\tilde{S}_{j}^{2m}\right)^{1/(2m)}
, this follows immediately from Lemma
S8.4
.
∎

Remark
S8.8
.

If
r=n^{a}
for some
a\in(0,m)
, then Corollary
S8.1
implies
\max_{j\in[r]}\lvert S_{j}\rvert=O_{P}(cn^{-\delta})
for
\delta=(1-a/m)/2\in(0,1/2)
.

Lemma
S8.5
.

Let
c>1
be a constant, and assume
\bm{e}_{g}\sim(\bm{0},\bm{V}_{g})
,
g\in[p]
, are independent sub-Gaussian random vectors with sub-Gaussian norm
\left\lVert\bm{e}_{g}\right\rVert_{\Psi_{2}}\leq c
. Then if
p\geq c^{-1}n
and for
\bm{E}=(\bm{e}_{1}\cdots\bm{e}_{p})
,
\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{E}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)
as
n,p\to\infty
.

Proof.

The proof is a simple extension of the proof of Theorem 5.39 in
[
53
]
, and has been omitted.
∎

Lemma
S8.6
.

Let
\bm{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
such that
\bm{M}_{gi}=w_{gi}-1
, and suppose Assumption
S8.4
hold. Then for any constant
\epsilon>0
,
\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{M}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
as
n,p\to\infty
.

Proof.

Conditional of
\bm{e}_{1},\ldots,\bm{e}_{p}
and
\bm{C}
, the entries of
\bm{M}
are mean 0, independent, and have finite fourth moments, where for any integer
m>0
and some constant
c_{m}>0
that only depends on
m
,

\displaystyle\E(w_{gi}^{m}\mid\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}},\bm{e}_{gi})=

\displaystyle\E([1/\Psi\{\alpha_{g}(y_{gi}-\delta_{g})\}]^{(m-1)}\mid\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}},\bm{e}_{gi})\leq c_{m}

\displaystyle+c_{m}\{\lvert\bm{e}_{gi}\rvert^{a(m-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lvert\bm{C}_{ik}\rvert^{a(m-1)}\}

for some constant
a>0
by
(h)
in Assumption
S8.4
. The result then follows by
[
54
]
and the fact that, for any
\epsilon>0
,
\max_{g\in[p],i\in[n]}\lvert\bm{e}_{gi}\rvert,\max_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}\lvert\bm{C}_{ik}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
.
∎

Lemma
S8.7
.

Let
\bm{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
such that
\bm{M}_{gi}=w_{gi}\bm{e}_{g_{i}}
, and suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds. Then for any fixed constant
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
,
\left\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{M}\right\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
as
n,p\to\infty
.

Proof.

We can express
\bm{M}
as
\bm{M}=\bm{M}^{(1)}+\bm{M}^{(2)}
for
\bm{M}^{(1)}_{gi}=\bm{e}_{gi}
and
\bm{M}^{(2)}_{gi}=(w_{gi}-1)\bm{e}_{g_{i}}
. By Lemma
S8.5
,
\lVert\bm{M}^{(1)}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)
, and a simple extension of the proof of Lemma
S8.6
can be used to show
\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{M}^{(2)}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
.
∎

Lemma
S8.8
.

Let
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}
be a matrix with orthonormal columns and define

\displaystyle f_{1}(\bm{U})=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}.

Let
\delta_{U}=\lVert\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}-\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\rVert_{2}
,
\eta\in(0,1/2)
be an arbitrarily small constant, and suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds. Then there exists a constant
c>1
that does not depend on
n
or
p
such that for all
\bm{U}
with
\delta_{U}\in(0,c^{-1})
and any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
,
f_{1}(\tilde{\bm{C}})-f_{1}(\bm{U})\geq c^{-1}\delta_{U}^{2}\{1-c\delta_{U}(1+\epsilon_{2}n^{-1/2+\eta})\}
with probability at least
1-\epsilon_{1}
for all
n,p
sufficiently large.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we set
\delta=\delta_{U}
. Let
\bm{U}=\bm{C}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}
for
\bm{Q}
as defined in Lemma
S8.3
, where
\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}\in[c^{-1}\delta,c\delta]
and
\left\lVert\bm{v}_{u}-\bm{v}\right\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta^{2}
for some constant
c>1
and
K\times K
unitary matrix
\bm{v}
. We let
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}=\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}
for the remainder of the proof, and without loos of generality, assume
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=I_{d}
. Provided
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}
is invertible, define

\displaystyle f_{1g}(\bm{U})=

\displaystyle\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr\{(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{-1/2}\times

(S8.4)

\displaystyle\times(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}\leq\Tr\{(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}=f_{1g}(\bm{C}),

where the inequality follows because the symmetric and positive semi-definite matrix in the second line has eigenvalues
\leq 1
. We first see that

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}.

Define the
K\times K
matrix
\bm{A}_{g}=\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}
. Then the expression inside the
\Tr
operator in (
S8.4
) can be written as

\displaystyle(\bm{A}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})(\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})^{-1}

\displaystyle\times(\bm{A}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})^{\top}=\bm{B}_{g}\{\bm{B}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\}^{-1}\bm{B}_{g}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{K}+\bm{D}_{g})^{-1}

(S8.5)

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{g}=\bm{A}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{g}=\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}.

(S8.6)

We first prove two lemmas that we will use throughout the proof.

Lemma
S8.9
.

Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds and let
\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}=\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\bm{B}_{g}
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
and some constant
c>0
that does not depend on
n
,
p
, or
\delta
,

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{A}_{g}-I_{K}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

(S8.7)

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{B}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}-I_{K}\rVert_{2},\,\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}-I_{K}\rVert_{2}\leq c(\delta+\delta^{2})\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}\text{ as $n,p\to\infty$.}

(S8.8)

Proof.

Define
\bm{R}=n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}
and let
\epsilon>0
be an arbitrarily small constant. Then

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{g}=\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}=

\displaystyle\bm{R}^{-1/2}\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C})\}\bm{R}^{-1/2},

where
\E\{\bm{R}^{-1/2}(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C})\bm{R}^{-1/2}\mid\bm{C}\}=I_{K}
and
\E(\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}\mid\bm{C})=\bm{0}
. First, Corollary
S8.1
implies
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}-n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
. Next,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}=n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}-n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}),

where a second application of Corollary
S8.1
shows that
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
. Next,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{C}

\displaystyle+(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{C})

\displaystyle-(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{C})

\displaystyle-\{(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{C})\}^{\top},

where further applications of Corollary
S8.1
to the terms in the above expression imply

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}-\bm{R}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

This proves (
S8.7
). Since
\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}=\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\bm{B}_{g}
, it suffices to only consider
\bm{B}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}
when proving (
S8.8
). We have

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}=\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}+(\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})^{\top}+\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}.

By Lemma
S8.3
and (
S8.7
),
\lVert\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}\bm{v}_{u}-I_{K}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta^{2}\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}
for some constant
c>0
. Since
\lVert\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta\lVert\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})^{2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\rVert_{2}^{1/2}
for some constant
c>0
, we need only show that
\lVert\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})^{2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\rVert_{2}\leq c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}
for some constant
c>0
to complete the proof. However, this follows from an identical analysis used to study the properties of
\bm{A}_{g}
, the details of which have been omitted.
∎

Lemma
S8.10
.

Suppose the assumptions of Lemma
S8.8
hold and let
\tilde{\bm{M}}=[n^{-1/2}\bm{X},\tilde{\bm{C}}]
. Then for any
a>0
, define
\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}=\diag[w_{g1}1\{w_{g1}>a\},\ldots,w_{gn}1\{w_{gn}>a\}]
. Then there exists constants
c>0
and
\eta_{a}>0
, the latter of which is a decreasing function of
a
, and a random variable
z=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
such that

\displaystyle(I_{K}+\bm{D}_{g})^{-1}\preceq

\displaystyle I_{K}+c\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}-\eta_{a}\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}

\displaystyle+\eta_{a}\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}+zI_{K}

Proof.

We assume
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=I_{d}
without loss of generality. Then we can express
\bm{D}_{g}
as

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{g}=

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{g}^{-T}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{N}_{g}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}

\displaystyle\bm{N}_{g}=

\displaystyle I_{n}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\bm{X}^{\top}-\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}=

\displaystyle I_{n}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\bm{X}^{\top}.

To simplify the expression for
\bm{N}_{g}
, we first see that

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}=\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}-(n^{-1/2}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\{n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\tilde{\bm{C}}\}.

Corollary
S8.1
can then be used to show that

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{X}\rVert_{2},\,\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}\rVert_{2}\leq 1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\tilde{\bm{C}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}),

which implies for
\tilde{\bm{X}}=n^{-1/2}\bm{X}
,

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{P}}_{g}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\bm{X}^{\top}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}\tilde{\bm{X}}^{\top}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Lemma
S8.9
can then be used to simplify show

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Putting this all together implies for
\tilde{\bm{M}}=[\tilde{\bm{X}},\tilde{\bm{C}}]
,

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{N}_{g}-(I_{n}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Therefore,
\bm{D}_{g}
satisfies

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{D}_{g}-\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}(I_{n}-\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2})\bm{W}_{g}^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}),

where for some constant
c>0

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}\rVert_{2}\leq\delta^{2}c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})\}.

Therefore, there exists a constant
\eta_{1}>0
and random variable
z=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
that does not depend on
g
such that

\displaystyle(I_{K}+\bm{D}_{g})^{-1}\preceq(I_{K}+\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1})^{-1}+\eta_{1}\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}+zI_{K}.

Next, let
a>0
be a constant and define
\bar{\bm{W}}_{g,a}=\diag[w_{g1}1\{w_{g1}\leq a\},\ldots,w_{gn}1\{w_{gn}\leq a\}]
. Then for some constant
\eta_{a}>0
that is a decreasing function of
a
,

\displaystyle(I_{K}+\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1})^{-1}\preceq(I_{K}+\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bar{\bm{W}}_{g,a}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1})^{-1}\preceq I_{K}-\eta_{a}\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bar{\bm{W}}_{g,a}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1},

which completes the proof.
∎

Returning to the proof of Lemma
S8.8
, let
a
,
\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}
, and
\tilde{\bm{M}}
be as given in the statement of Lemma
S8.10
and define

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}=\bm{A}_{g}^{-1/2}\bm{B}_{g}=\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{A}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{g}=\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{g}=\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1},\quad\bm{R}_{g,a}=\bm{B}_{g}^{-\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{B}_{g}^{-1}.

Then for constants
c
and
\eta_{a}
as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.10
, Lemma
S8.10
implies the objective can be lower bounded as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} f_{1g}(\bm{C})-f_{1g}(\bm{U})\geq&\eta_{a}\Tr\{\bm{R}_{g}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}\\
&-\eta_{a}\Tr\{\bm{R}_{g,a}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}\\
&-c\Tr\{\bm{S}_{g}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}+O_{P}(\lambda n^{-1/2+\epsilon}),\end{aligned}

(S8.9)

where the error term
O_{P}(\lambda n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
is uniform over
g\in[p]
. For the third term in (
S8.9
),

\displaystyle\bm{M}^{(1)}_{g}

\displaystyle=\Tr\{\bm{S}_{g}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}\leq\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\Tr\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}(\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g})^{-1}\}

\displaystyle\leq c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}

for some constant
c>0
that does not depend on
n
or
p
, where the second inequality holds by Lemma
S8.9
for
\delta
small enough. Note that the by Lemma
S8.9
, the
O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
term is uniform over
g\in[p]
. Define
s_{g}^{2}=\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\leq c\lambda\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})\}
, where the error is uniform over
g\in[p]
. Then

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{M}^{(1)}_{g}

\displaystyle\leq c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\left\{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}s_{g}^{2}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}\tilde{\bm{M}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\right\}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}

\displaystyle=c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{r=1}^{d+K}(\lambda p)^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\sum_{g=1}^{p}s_{g}^{2}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}.

We see that

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\sum_{g=1}^{p}s_{g}^{2}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}=

\displaystyle p^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{S}\bm{G}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}

\displaystyle\bm{G}=

\displaystyle[\bm{G}_{1}\cdots\bm{G}_{p}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p},\quad\bm{G}_{g}=\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}

\displaystyle\bm{S}=

\displaystyle\diag(s_{1}^{2}/\lambda,\ldots,s_{p}^{2}/\lambda),

where by Cauchy-Schwarz and the fact that
\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{2}\leq c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})\}
for some constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle 0\leq(\lambda p)^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}^{\top}\sum_{g=1}^{p}s_{g}^{2}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}\leq c\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})\}(p^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}})

for some constant
c>0
. Let
\tilde{\bm{S}}=p^{-1}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{\bm{S}}_{ij}=&\tilde{\bm{M}}_{ir}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{jr}p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(w_{gi}-1)(w_{gj}-1)+\tilde{\bm{M}}_{ir}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{jr}p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}w_{gi}+\tilde{\bm{M}}_{ir}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{jr}p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}w_{gj}\\
&-\tilde{\bm{M}}_{ir}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{jr}.\end{aligned}

(S8.10)

Since
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}k}}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}=0
for all
k\in[K]
and
r\in[d+K]
, the last three terms in (
S8.10
) are nullified, implying we need only study the first term. We then have that for
\bm{M}=\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
such that
\bm{M}_{ig}=w_{gi}-1
,
\tilde{\bm{S}}=p^{-1}\diag(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r})\bm{M}\bm{M}^{\top}\diag(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r})
. By Lemma
S8.6
,
\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{M}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
for an arbitrarily small constant
\epsilon>0
. Therefore,
\lVert\tilde{\bm{S}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon}\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{ir}^{2})=O_{P}(n^{-1+\epsilon})
, which implies
(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{M}_{g}^{(1)}=O_{P}(\delta^{2}n^{-1+\epsilon})
.

We next consider the first term in (
S8.9
). Here,

\displaystyle\Tr\{\bm{R}_{g}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}=\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-T}).

Since
f_{1g}(\bm{U})
in (
S8.4
) only depends on
\im(\bm{U})
, it suffices to assume
\lVert I_{K}-\bm{v}_{u}\rVert_{2}=O(\delta^{2})
. Let
\bm{\Delta}_{g}=I_{K}-\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}
, where an identical analysis to that used to prove (
S8.8
) in Lemma
S8.9
can be used to show that
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq c(\delta+\delta^{2})\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}
. Next,

\displaystyle\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-T})=

\displaystyle\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top})-\Tr\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}(\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top})\}

\displaystyle+\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top})-\Tr\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}(\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top})\}.

For
\bm{R}=n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{X}^{\perp}\bm{C}
,

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top})=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}n\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{R}^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}\bm{R}^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}n\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})+O_{P}(\delta^{2}n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}n\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\geq

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lambda_{K}/\lambda+\sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{ir}}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{is}}\underbrace{p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{gr}\bm{\ell}_{gs}/\lambda)(w_{gi}-1)}_{=x_{irs}}.

Since
\{w_{gi}-1\}_{g\in[p]}
are mean 0 and independent conditional on
\bm{C}
and
\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert n\bm{\ell}_{gr}\bm{\ell}_{gs}/\lambda\rvert\leq c
for some constant
c>0
, Corollary
S8.1
implies

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[n],\,r,s\in[K]\end{subarray}}\left\lvert x_{irs}\right\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Lastly,
\max_{g\in[p]}s_{g}^{-2}\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}
for some constant
c>0
and
\delta
small enough, meaning

\displaystyle\lvert\Tr\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}(\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top})\}\rvert\leq c\delta\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}s_{g}^{2}\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})

for some constant
c>0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}(\bm{\Delta}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}_{g}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{g}^{\top})\}\rvert\leq c\delta^{3}\{1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}

for some constant
c>0
.

We lastly consider the second term in (
S8.9
). For
a>0
as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.10
,
\E[w_{gi}1\{w_{gi}>a\}]\leq\epsilon_{a}
. for some constant
\epsilon_{a}\geq 0
that is a non-increasing function of
a
, and can be made arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
c>0
and random variable
z=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
that does not depend on
g
such that

\displaystyle\bm{M}_{g}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle\Tr\{\bm{R}_{g,a}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{1/2}\}\leq(c+z)\lambda\Tr(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u})\leq\delta^{2}(c+z)\lambda\epsilon_{a}

\displaystyle+(c+z)\lambda\Tr[\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\{\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}-\E(\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a})\}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}].

We see that

\displaystyle p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr[\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\{\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a}-\E(\tilde{\bm{W}}_{g,a})\}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u_{ik}}^{2}p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(w_{gi}1\{w_{gi}>a\}-\E[w_{gi}1\{w_{gi}>a\}]),

where

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\lvert p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(w_{gi}1\{w_{gi}>a\}-\E[w_{gi}1\{w_{gi}>a\}])\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Choosing
a>0
large enough, and therefore
\epsilon_{a}\geq 0
small enough, thus completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.11
.

Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds and let
\Omega_{\delta}=\{\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}:\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{U}=I_{K},\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{0},\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\delta\}
. Then for all
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
,
\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\rVert_{2}\leq c+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
.

Proof.

For
\bm{v}_{u}
and
\bm{z}_{u}
as defined in Lemma
S8.3
, Lemma
S8.3
implies that for some constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\succeq(1-\delta^{2}c)I_{K}+\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}-I_{K})\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}+(\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u})^{\top}.

By Lemma
S8.9
and the proof of (
S8.8
) in Lemma
S8.9
,

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}-I_{K}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq\{c+O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})\}\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}

for some constant
c>0
. Since
\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\left\lVert\bm{z}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}=O(\delta)
by Lemma
S8.3
, this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.12
.

Define
f_{3}(\bm{U})=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}
and suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds. Then for any constant
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}_{\delta}\in\Omega}f_{3}(\bm{U})=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon}),\quad\Omega_{\delta}=\{\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}:\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{U}=I_{K},\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{0},\left\lVert P_{\bm{U}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\delta\}

for all
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
be an arbitrarily small constant. For any
\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle f_{3}(\bm{U})\leq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{U}\}

\displaystyle-2c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}\underbrace{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{U}\}}_{=S(\bm{U})}

\displaystyle+c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}\underbrace{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{U}\}}_{=T(\bm{U})}

by Lemma
S8.11
for
\delta>0
sufficiently small. By Lemma
S8.7
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{U}\}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon}).

Since
f_{3}(\bm{U})
only depends on
\bm{X}
through
\im(\bm{X})
, it suffices to assume
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=I_{d}
, where by
(a)
of Assumption
S8.4
, the entries of
\bm{X}
are uniformly bounded from above and below. Define
\bm{\Delta}_{g}=I_{d}-(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}
. By Corollary
S8.1
,
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
and

\displaystyle S(\bm{U})=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\Tr\left(\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{s=1}^{d}\bm{A}_{s}\bm{U}\right)+(\lambda p)^{-1}\Tr\left(\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{r,s=1}^{d}\bm{B}_{rs}\bm{U}\right)

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{s}=

\displaystyle\diag(\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s})\bm{W}^{\top}\diag(n^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{1}\bm{e}_{1},\ldots,n^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{p}\bm{e}_{p})\tilde{\bm{E}},\quad s\in[d]

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{rs}=

\displaystyle\diag(\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r})\bm{W}^{\top}\diag(n^{-1}\bm{\Delta}_{1_{rs}}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{1}\bm{e}_{1},\ldots,n^{-1}\bm{\Delta}_{p_{rs}}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{p}\bm{e}_{p})\tilde{\bm{E}},\quad r,s\in[d]

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{gi}=

\displaystyle w_{gi}-1,\quad\tilde{\bm{E}}_{gi}=\bm{e_{g_{i}}}w_{gi},\quad g\in[p];i\in[n].

Since the entries of
\bm{X}
are uniformly bounded,
\lVert\diag(\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s})\rVert_{2}=O(1)
for all
s\in[d]
. By Lemmas
S8.6
and
S8.7
,
\lVert p^{-1/2}\bm{W}\rVert_{2},\lVert p^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{E}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
, and by Corollary
S8.1
,

\displaystyle\lVert\diag(n^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{1}\bm{e}_{1},\ldots,n^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s}^{\top}\bm{W}_{p}\bm{e}_{p})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

Putting this all together implies
\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega}S(\bm{U})=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1}n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
. An identical analysis can be used to show that
\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega}T(\bm{U})=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1}n^{-1+\epsilon})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.13
.

Define
f_{2}(\bm{U})=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}
. Then under the assumptions of Lemma
S8.12
,
\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\lvert f_{2}(\bm{U})\rvert=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})
for any constant
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

Proof.

Let
\bm{U}\in\Omega
and define
a_{g_{rs}}
to be the
r,s
element of
(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1/2}
for
(r,s)\in[K]\times[K]
. Note that
\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert a_{g_{rs}}\rvert\leq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}
for some constant
c>0
by Lemma
S8.11
. Then for
\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{g}=\lambda^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}
, where
\max_{g\in[p]}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\leq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}
for some constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle f_{2}(\bm{U})=

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1/2}\sum_{r,s=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{B}_{rs}\bm{U}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}s},\quad\bm{A}=p^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{1}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{1}^{\perp}\\
\vdots\\
\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{p}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{p}^{\perp}\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{B}_{rs}=p^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}a_{1_{rs}}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}\bm{P}_{1}^{\perp}\\
\vdots\\
a_{p_{rs}}\bm{e}_{p}^{\top}\bm{P}_{p}^{\perp}\end{pmatrix},

where
\lVert\bm{A}\rVert_{2},\lVert\bm{B}_{rs}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
by the proofs of Lemmas
S8.8
and
S8.12
.
∎

Corollary
S8.2
.

Suppose the assumptions of Lemma
S8.12
hold and let
\Omega_{\delta}
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.12
. Then for
f
defined in (
S8.1
),
\hat{\bm{C}}=\argmax_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}f(\bm{U})
, and
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists a constant
\eta\in(0,1/4)
such that
\lVert P_{\hat{\bm{C}}}-P_{\tilde{\bm{C}}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
as
n,p\to\infty
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemmas
S8.8
,
S8.12
, and
S8.13
.
∎

S8.4
Properties and rate of convergence of
\hat{\bm{C}}

Here we study the properties and rate of convergence of
\hat{\bm{C}}
. To do so, we use the decomposition discussed in Lemma
S8.3
, where any
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}
such that
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{U}=I_{K}
can be expressed as
\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{Q}\bm{z}_{u}
, where the columns of
\bm{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(n-K)}
form an orthonormal basis for
\ker(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top})
,
\bm{v}_{u},\bm{z}_{u}
depend on
\bm{U}
, and
\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{z}_{u}^{\top}\bm{z}_{u}=I_{K}
. We can therefore write
\hat{\bm{C}}
defined in the statement of Corollary
S8.2
as
\hat{\bm{C}}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\hat{\bm{v}}+\bm{Q}\hat{\bm{z}}
, where to understand the properties of
\hat{\bm{C}}
, we need only determine
\hat{\bm{v}}
and
\hat{\bm{z}}
.

Define
\tilde{f}\{(\bm{v}^{\top}\,\bm{z}^{\top})^{\top}\}=f(\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}+\bm{Q}\bm{z})
, where
f
is as defined in (
S8.1
). Then for
\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\bm{Q}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\bm{s}}\{(\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\,\bm{z}_{u}^{\top})^{\top}\}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\\
\bm{Q}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\nabla_{\bm{U}}f(\bm{U})\\
=&(p\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\\
\bm{Q}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\{\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1},\end{aligned}

(S8.11)

where for any unitary matrix
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\bm{s}}\{(\bm{v}^{\top}\,\bm{0})^{\top}\}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{K\times K}\\
(p\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{Q}^{\top}\{\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bm{v}\\
&+\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{K\times K}\\
(p\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{Q}^{\top}\{\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}(\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\bm{v}.\end{aligned}

(S8.12)

The Hessian can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{\bm{H}}(\bm{U})=&\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})^{\top}\}\nabla_{\bm{U}}^{2}f(\bm{U})\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})\}\\
=&(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})^{\top}\}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\otimes\{\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})\}\\
&-(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})^{\top}\}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\otimes\{\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\bm{\Pi}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})\}\\
&-(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})^{\top}\}\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})\}\\
&-(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})^{\top}\}\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{\Pi}\{I_{K}\otimes(\tilde{\bm{C}}\,\bm{Q})\}\end{aligned}

(S8.13)

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})=\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp},\quad\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})=\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1},

where
\bm{\Pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{nK\times nK}
is a permutation matrix that satisfies
\bm{\Pi}\myvec(\bm{U})=\myvec(\bm{U}^{\top})
for
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K}
. We next prove a series of lemmas that facilitate understanding
\tilde{\bm{s}}
and
\tilde{\bm{H}}
, and will lead to an exact expression for
\hat{\bm{C}}-\tilde{\bm{C}}
.

Lemma
S8.14

(First term in (
S8.13
))
.

Define
\bm{H}_{g}^{(1)}(\bm{U})=(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\otimes\{\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\}
, suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, let
\eta\in(0,1/2)
, and let
\hat{\bm{C}}
be as defined in Corollary
S8.2
. Then for
\delta=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
and any constant
\epsilon\in(0,\eta)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{H}_{g}^{(1)}(\bm{U})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-2\eta+\epsilon}+\lambda^{-1+\epsilon}).

Proof.

By Lemma
S8.11
,
(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\preceq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}I_{K}
for some constant
c>0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{H}_{g}^{(1)}(\bm{U})\preceq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}I_{K}\otimes\left[(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\right].

First,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{y}_{g}=&\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}+\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{e}_{g}\\
\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}=&\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\\
\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{e}_{g}=&\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}-\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}.\end{aligned}

(S8.14)

By the proof of Lemma
S8.12
,

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon}).

Next, since
\delta=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
, it is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}}\max_{g\in[p]}\bm{U}^{T}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})^{2}\bm{U}\leq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}

for some constant
c>0
, which implies

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{T}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{T}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{T}(\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp})^{2}\bm{U}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}.

where the
o_{P}(1)
error term is uniform over all
\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}
. The proof of Lemma
S8.12
shows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\Tr\{\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}\}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})

and implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon}).

For the remaining term in (
S8.14
), let
\bm{U}=\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}+\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}
, where
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\in\ker([\bm{X},\tilde{\bm{C}}]^{\top})
and
\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}+\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}=I_{K}
. By Lemma
S8.3
,
\lVert\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\bm{v}_{u}-I_{K}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta^{2}
and
\lVert\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta
for some constant
c>0
. Therefore, since
\delta=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
and by the proof of (
S8.8
) of Lemma
S8.9
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U}-\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq c\delta\{1+o_{P}(1)\}

for some constant
c>0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}=(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{v}_{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}+(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{U})^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{C}}-\bm{v}_{u}^{-1}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-\eta}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\tilde{\bm{C}}\rVert_{2}\leq O_{P}(n^{-\eta})+\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\max_{g\in[p]}\left\lVert\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-\eta+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon\in(0,\eta)
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.15

(Third term in (
S8.13
))
.

Suppose the assumptions of Lemma
S8.14
hold, let
\eta\in(0,1/2)
, and let
\Omega_{\delta}
be as defined in Lemma
S8.13
. Then if
\delta=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
, there exists a unitary matrix
\bm{v}=\bm{v}(\bm{U})\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
that depends on
\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{U}\in\Omega_{\delta}}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})-(\lambda^{-1}\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{v})\otimes\bm{Q}\bm{Q}^{\top}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-\eta+\epsilon}+\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})

for any constant
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

We see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{y}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})=&\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})+\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\\
&+\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})+\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}^{\top}.\end{aligned}

(S8.15)

First, since
\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\preceq\bm{W}_{g}
,

\displaystyle\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{A}_{g}(\bm{U})\preceq\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{W}_{g},

where

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}\otimes\bm{W}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}w_{gi}\Tr\{\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{B}_{g}(\bm{U})\}.

Identical techniques to those used to prove Lemma
S8.12
can be used to show that
