, absolute values of the third through fifth terms in (
S8.32
) are all
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})
. The first term in (
S8.32
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}\bm{W}_{h}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}+(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{h}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}

\displaystyle+(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}+(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{h}-I_{n})(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}.

Since
\bm{e}_{g}
is sub-Gaussian random vector with independent entries and uniformly sub-Gaussian norm, Corollary
S8.1
implies
\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
. For the second term, we see that for
c_{hr}=\lambda^{-1/2}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}
,

\displaystyle x_{gr}=p^{-1}\lambda^{-1/2}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{h}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}=p^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{hr}\bm{e}_{hi}\bm{e}_{gi}(w_{hi}-1).

Since
\max_{(h,g)\in[p]\times[p];i\in[n]}\E[\{c_{hr}\bm{e}_{hi}\bm{e}_{gi}(w_{hi}-1)\}^{2m}]
is bounded from above by a constant that only depends on
m>0
and the elements of
\{w_{hi}-1\}_{h\in[p]\setminus\{g\};i\in[n]}
are mean 0 and independent conditional on
\{\bm{E},\bm{C}\}
, Lemma
S8.4
implies
\max_{g\in[p]}\E(x_{gr}^{2m})
is bounded above by a constant that only depends on
m>0
. Corollary
S8.1
therefore implies
\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{h}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
. Further applications of Lemma
S8.4
and Corollary
S8.1
can be used to show

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rvert,\,\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{h}-I_{n})(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. This implies the first term in (
S8.32
) satisfies

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lvert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{h\neq g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{hr}\bm{e}_{h}^{\top}\bm{W}_{h}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\ \rvert=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
, which gives us that (
S8.31
) satisfies

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\bm{s}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}r}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})

(S8.33)

for any
\epsilon>0
. The expression for
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
in (
S8.28
) and the maximal inequalities in (
S8.29
), (
S8.30
), and (
S8.33
) imply

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}-\{\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}+\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}+(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}+(\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v})^{\top}\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{\epsilon})

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}-\{\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}+\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}+(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}+(\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top})^{\top}\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. To complete the proof, we need only show that
\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}
and
\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}
are both
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
. Let
\bm{R}=(n^{-1}\bm{C}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{1/2}
and assume
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=I_{d}
without loss of generality. Then for
\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{g}=\lambda^{-1/2}n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g}
and
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}\leq\underbrace{\lVert\bm{R}\rVert_{2}\lVert\bm{R}^{-1}\rVert_{2}}_{=O_{P}(1)}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g})^{\top}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{gk})=\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\\
&-\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\end{aligned}

(S8.34)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}=\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\\
&+\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\\
&-(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}.\end{aligned}

(S8.35)

The first term is
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2}p^{-1/2})=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1})
by Lemma
S8.2
and Remark
S8.7
. For the second term,

\displaystyle\V\{p^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(n^{-1/2}\bm{C})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\}=p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}^{2}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\{(w_{gi}-1)^{2}\bm{e}_{gi}^{2}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\}\preceq cI_{K}

for some
c>0
, meaning the second term in (
S8.35
) is
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1})
. An identical analysis shows that the third term in (
S8.35
) is also
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1})
, which proves the first term in (
S8.34
) is
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1})
. For the second term in (
S8.34
), we first see that

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}=n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}-(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})\{n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\rVert_{2},\,\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. And since
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
, the second term in (
S8.34
) is
O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
, which proves
\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
. Lastly,

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}\leq\underbrace{\lVert\bm{R}\rVert_{2}}_{=O_{P}(1)}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g})^{\top}\rVert_{2},

where for
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{gk})=\lambda^{-1/2}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}+\lambda^{-1/2}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}

\displaystyle-\lambda^{-1/2}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\{n^{-1/2}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}.

We first see that

\displaystyle\E\left(\lVert p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)=p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}^{2}p^{-1}\Tr\{\V(\bm{e}_{g})\}\leq c

for some constant
c>0
. Next,

\displaystyle\E\left\{\lVert p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right\}=p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}p^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\{(w_{gi}-1)^{2}\bm{e}_{gi}^{2}\}\leq c

for some constant
c>0
. Since

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\{n^{-1/2}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon}),

this implies

\displaystyle\lVert p^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\{n^{-1/2}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{gk}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. Since
\lVert\bm{\Delta}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
by Theorem
S8.1
,

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum\limits_{g=1}^{p}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{P}_{g}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
, which completes the proof.
∎

S8.5
Properties of our estimate for
\bm{\Omega}

Theorem
S8.2
.

Suppose the Assumptions of Theorem
S8.1
hold, let
\bm{\Omega}=(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{-1/2}\bm{C}^{\top}\allowbreak\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}
, and for
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{g}^{\naive}=(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}
and
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
as defined in the statement of Corollary
S8.7
, define
\hat{\bm{\Omega}}=n^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left[\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\{\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{g}^{\naive}\}^{\top}\right]
. Then
\lVert\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}-\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
for
\bm{v}
as defined in the statement of Theorem
S8.1
and all
j\in[d_{1}]
.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{g}^{\naive}=\bm{\beta}_{g}+n^{-1/2}\bm{\Omega}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}+(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\tilde{\bm{C}}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}+(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}.

Define
\hat{\bm{S}}=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}
, where
\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)
by Corollary
S8.7
. Then for
j\in[d_{1}]
and
\bm{a}_{j}
the
j
th standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}=&\hat{\bm{S}}^{-1}\{n^{1/2}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{\beta}_{gj}\}+\hat{\bm{S}}^{-1}\{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}\\
&+\hat{\bm{S}}^{-1}[(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\{\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{a}_{j}]\\
&+\hat{\bm{S}}^{-1}\{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\bm{a}_{j}\}.\end{aligned}

(S8.36)

By Corollary
S8.7
,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{-1}\{(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}-\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

For the fourth term in (
S8.36
),

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\rVert_{2}\underbrace{\lVert(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}}_{=O(1)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})\{(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}-(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\}\rVert_{2}}_{=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}-\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g})(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}}_{=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})},

where the second and third lines follow because
\lVert\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}-\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})
by Corollary
S8.7
and
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}-(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
. Next,

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})

\displaystyle+(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})

\displaystyle\V\{(\lambda p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j})\}=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\{n^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}^{\top}\V(\bm{e}_{g})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}\preceq cI_{K}

\displaystyle\V\{(\lambda p)^{-1/2}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j})\}=

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}[n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\{(w_{gi}-1)^{2}\bm{e}_{gi}^{2}\bm{X}_{ij}^{2}\}]

\displaystyle\preceq

\displaystyle cI_{K}

for some constant
c>0
and all
j\in[d]
, which implies

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

For the third term, we first see that

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})=\lambda^{-1}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g})^{\top}\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}

\displaystyle-\lambda^{-1}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}(n^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g})^{\top}(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\{n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\},

which implies

\displaystyle\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\lambda^{-1}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. Consequently, for
\bm{R}=(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{1/2}
and
j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\{\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\lVert\bm{R}\rVert_{2}}_{=O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}}_{=O(1)}\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}\}\rVert_{2}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}=(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}

\displaystyle-(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})\{n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}.

Define
\bm{S}_{g}=n\lambda^{-1}\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}
, which has uniformly bounded entries. Then

\displaystyle(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}=(np)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(w_{gi}-1)\bm{S}_{g}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}\bm{X}_{ij}

\displaystyle\V\{(np)^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(w_{gi}-1)\bm{S}_{g}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}\bm{X}_{ij}\}=p^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{[n^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\bm{X}_{ij}^{2}\bm{S}_{g}\E\{(w_{gi}-1)^{2}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\}\bm{S}_{g}]}_{\preceq cI_{K}}

for some constant
c>0
, which implies

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})\{n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

As an identical analysis can be used to show that

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}(n\bm{\ell}_{g}\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{X})(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})\{n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{X}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

the third term in (
S8.36
) satisfies

\displaystyle\lVert(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\tilde{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\{\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})\}(n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{X})^{-1}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

For the first term in (
S8.36
), we note that
\max_{g\in[p]}\lVert\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq\lambda^{1/2}c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}
for some constant
c>0
. Therefore, for some constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\lVert n^{1/2}(\lambda p)^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\bm{\beta}_{gj}\rVert_{2}\leq c\{1+o_{P}(1)\}(n/\lambda)^{1/2}\{p^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{p}I(\bm{\beta}_{gj}\neq 0)\}=o_{P}(n^{1/2}),\quad j\in[d_{1}]

by Assumption
S8.4
, which completes the proof.
∎

Corollary
S8.8
.

In addition to the assumptions of Theorem
S8.2
, suppose
\E(\bm{C}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bm{X}_{ij}\bm{\omega}_{j}
for
\bm{\omega}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
. Then for a fixed
j\in[d_{1}]
and
Z\sim\chi_{K}^{2}
,
[\{(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})^{-1}\}_{jj}]^{-1}\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}j}\edist Z+o_{P}(1)
if
\bm{\omega}_{j}=\bm{0}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from Theorem
S8.2
and the proof of Theorem 3 in
[
35
]
. The details have been omitted.
∎

S8.6
Estimating coefficients in differential abundance analyses

For notational convenience, we let
\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}=P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}}
be the estimator obtained from (
S8.1
) for the remainder of the supplement. Note that by construction,
\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}^{\top}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}=I_{K}
.

Lemma
S8.20
.

Let
\hat{\bm{C}}=n^{1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}+P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1}\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}^{\top}
and
\hat{\bm{Z}}=[P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1},\hat{\bm{C}},\bm{X}_{2}]
for
\bm{X}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{j}}
,
j=1,2
, given in Assumption
S8.4
and
\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times d_{1}}
the first
d_{1}
columns
\hat{\bm{\Omega}}
defined in the statement of Theorem
S8.2
. Define the inverse probability weighted (IPW) estimator

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\ipw}=(\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}})^{-1}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}

and the parameter vector

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}=(\bm{\beta}_{g1}^{\top},\{\bm{v}^{\top}(n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{C})^{1/2}\bm{\ell}_{g}\}^{\top},\{\bm{\beta}_{g2}+(\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\bm{X}_{2})^{-1}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{1}\bm{\beta}_{g1}+\bm{C}\bm{\ell}_{g})\}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K},

where
\bm{\beta}_{g1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
and
\bm{\beta}_{g2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
are the first
d_{1}
and last
d_{2}
elements of
\bm{\beta}_{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}+d_{2}}
. Then under the assumptions of Theorem
S8.2
,
\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\ipw}-\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.

Proof.

Note that
\E(\bm{y}_{g})=\bm{X}_{1}\bm{\beta}_{g1}+\bm{C}\bm{\ell}_{g}+\bm{X}_{2}\bm{\beta}_{g2}=\bm{Z}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
for
\bm{Z}=[\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}+\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1}\bm{\Omega}_{1}^{\top}\bm{v},\bm{X_{2}}]
,
\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1}=P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1}
, and
\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.20
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\ipw}-\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}=

\displaystyle(n^{-1}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}})^{-1}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{Z})\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}+(n^{-1}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}})^{-1}(n^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})

\displaystyle+(n^{-1}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}})^{-1}(n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g})

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}=

\displaystyle[\bm{0}_{n\times d_{1}},\tilde{\bm{C}}(\hat{\bm{v}}-\bm{v})+\bm{Q}\hat{\bm{z}}+(n^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1})(\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{\Omega}_{1}^{\top}\bm{v}),\bm{0}_{n\times d_{2}}].

First, identical techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.6
can be used to show
\lVert n^{-1}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)
. Next,

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lVert n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2})+O_{P}(\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2})+O_{P}(\lVert n^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}),

where the first term is trivially
O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. For the second,

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}-(n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s_{g}*}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s_{g}*})\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{s_{g}g}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{s_{g}*}+\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{s_{g}g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\lVert\bm{\gamma}^{(e)}_{s_{g}g}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{s_{g}*}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
by Assumption
S8.4
. Therefore,
\lVert n^{-1}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. For the third term, there exists an
\bm{R}
such that
\bm{Z}=[\bm{X},\bm{C}]\bm{R}
and
\lVert\bm{R}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(1)
, meaning

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq O_{P}\{\lVert n^{-1}[\bm{X},\bm{C}]^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}\},

where for
\bar{\bm{c}}_{i}=[\bm{X},\bm{C}]_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}
,

\displaystyle\V\{n^{-1/2}[\bm{X},\bm{C}]^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\{(w_{gi}-1)^{2}\bm{e}_{gi}^{2}\bar{\bm{c}}_{i}\bar{\bm{c}}_{i}^{\top}\}\preceq cI_{d+K}

for some constant
c>0
. This proves
\lVert n^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. We next see that by Theorems
S8.1
and
S8.2
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{Z})\rVert_{2}=\lVert\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{Z})\rVert_{2}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

where identical techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.6
can be used to show
\lVert\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{Z})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. A second application of Theorems
S8.1
and
S8.2
imply

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

where techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.7
can be used to show
\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.
∎

Lemma
S8.21
.

Fix a
g\in[p]
and suppose the assumptions of Lemma
S8.20
hold, let
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\IPW}
and
\hat{\bm{Z}}
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.20
, and let
\{\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\IPW}\}^{2}=(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{gi})^{-1}\allowbreak\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{gi}\allowbreak\{y_{gi}-\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i*}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{\IPW}\}^{2}
. Then
\lvert\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\IPW}-\sigma_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.

Proof.

Since
\{w_{gi}\}_{i\in[n]}
are independent with uniformly bounded
m
moments for all non-negative
m
,
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{gi}=1+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. Next,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i*}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\IPW}\}^{2}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\{\bm{e}_{g}+\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}-\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\{\bm{e}_{g}+\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}-\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}+n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}+2n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}

\displaystyle-2n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}+n^{-1}\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}

\displaystyle-2n^{-1}\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}+n^{-1}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}^{\top}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}

for
\bm{\delta}=\bm{Z}-\hat{\bm{Z}}
and
\bm{\epsilon}_{g}=\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\IPW}-\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
. Note that
\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
by Lemma
S8.20
. Going through each of the above seven terms, it is easy to see that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{e}_{g}-\sigma_{g}^{2}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}),\quad\lvert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}(\bm{W}_{g}-I_{n})\bm{e}_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lvert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rvert\leq\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{e}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{Z}}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(n^{-1/2})}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lvert n^{-1}\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rvert\leq\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}^{2}}_{O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon/2})}\underbrace{\lVert\bm{W}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(n^{\epsilon/2})}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1+\epsilon})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})}\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{W}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(n^{-1/2})}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}^{\top}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}\leq\underbrace{\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{g}\rVert_{2}^{2}}_{O_{P}(n^{-1})}\underbrace{\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{Z}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}\underbrace{\lVert\bm{W}_{g}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(n^{\epsilon})}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

The proof will be complete if we can show
\lVert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
, where

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1})\rVert_{2}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}\rVert_{2}}_{O_{P}(1)}+\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}\rVert_{2}

for
\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}
and
\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}
as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.20
. It is easy to see
\lVert n^{-1}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}(P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1})\rVert_{2}\allowbreak=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. And since we showed
n^{-1/2}\bm{e}_{g}^{\top}\bm{W}_{g}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
in the proof of Lemma
S8.20
, the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma
S8.22
.

Let
\tilde{\bm{C}}
and
\bm{v}
be as defined in Theorem
S8.1
and
\bm{\Omega}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times d_{1}}
be the first
d_{1}
columns of
\bm{\Omega}
defined in Theorem
S8.2
. Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, fix a
g\in[p]
, let
\bm{Z}=[P_{\bm{X}_{2}}\bm{X}_{1},n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}+P_{\bm{X}_{2}}\bm{X}_{1}\bm{\Omega}_{1}^{\top}\bm{v},\bm{X}_{2}]
and
\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.20
, let
\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}=(\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top},\sigma_{g}^{2})^{\top}
, and for some constant
\delta>0
small enough, define the maximum likelihood estimator

\displaystyle\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}=

\displaystyle\argmax_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=

\displaystyle\{\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{K+d}:\lVert\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}\leq\delta\},\quad\mathcal{S}=\{\sigma>0:\lvert\sigma-\sigma_{g}\rvert\leq\delta\}

\displaystyle f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-r_{gi}\{\bm{y}_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle+(1-r_{gi})\log\left(\smallint\phi(\epsilon)\Psi[-\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\sigma\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\text{d}\epsilon\right),\quad\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})=\bm{Z}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}.

Then for
\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}=(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})^{\top}
and
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\known}=(\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{\known}_{g},\hat{\sigma}^{\known}_{g})^{\top}
,
\lVert\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\known}-\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
,
\{n\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\}^{1/2}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\known}-\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}\}\edist\bm{V}_{g}+o_{P}(1)
, and
\lVert\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}-\E\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\mid\bm{C},\bm{G}\}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
, where

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{g}\sim N_{K+d+1}(\bm{0},I_{K+d+1}),\quad\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}=-\nabla^{2}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g}).

Remark
S8.10
.

Proposition
5.2
follows from Lemma
S8.22
by letting
\bm{X}=\bm{X}_{1}
and
\bm{X}_{2}=0
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that there exists a change-of-basis matrix
\hat{\bm{R}}
that depends on
\bm{C}
such that
\bm{Z}=[\bm{X},\bm{C}]\hat{\bm{R}}
,
\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}=\hat{\bm{R}}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{g}^{\top},\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})^{\top}
, and
\lVert\hat{\bm{R}}-\bm{R}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
for some non-random
\bm{R}
with
\lVert\bm{R}\rVert_{2}\leq c
and
\lVert\bm{R}^{-1}\rVert_{2}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
. Therefore, it suffices to prove the theorem assuming
\bm{Z}=[\bm{X},\bm{C}]
and
\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}=(\bm{\beta}_{g}^{\top},\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top})^{\top}
. Let
\gamma=\bm{\gamma}_{s_{g}g}^{(e)}
and define

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-r_{gi}\{\bm{y}_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})-\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\gamma\}^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle+(1-r_{gi})\log\left(\smallint\phi(\epsilon)\Psi[-\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\gamma+\sigma\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\text{d}\epsilon\right).

Then for
h(\mu,\sigma)=\log\left(\smallint\phi(\epsilon)\Psi[-\alpha_{g}\{\mu+\sigma\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\text{d}\epsilon\right)
,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle 2\gamma n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\{\bm{y}_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle-\gamma^{2}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h(\tilde{\mu}_{i},\sigma),

where
\tilde{\mu}_{i}=\alpha_{i}\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\alpha_{i})\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}
for some
\alpha_{i}\in[0,1]
. Since
\sup_{i\in[n]}\lvert\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\rvert=o(n^{-1/4})
,
\sup_{i\in[n],\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h(\tilde{\mu}_{i},\sigma)\rvert\leq c
for some constant
c>0
by Lemma
S8.25
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert=o_{P}(n^{-1/4}).

Next, define
F_{g}(\bm{\theta})=f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta})-\E\{\tilde{f}_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta})\mid\bm{C},\bm{G}\}
. Then
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}
are consistent if
\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert F_{g}(\bm{\theta})\rvert=o_{P}(1)
. We see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert F_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert\leq&\underbrace{\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert}_{o_{P}(n^{-1/4})}\\
&+\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\lvert\underbrace{\tilde{f}_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\E\{\tilde{f}_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\mid\bm{C},\bm{G}\}}_{=\tilde{F}_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)}\rvert.\end{aligned}

(S8.37)

Since
\E[\lvert\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\rvert^{m}]\leq c_{m}
for some constant
c_{m}
that only depends on
m>0
,
\lvert\tilde{F}_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert=o_{P}(1)
for all
\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}
. Next, let
\bm{R}_{gi}=\diag(r_{g1},\ldots,r_{gn})
and
B(\bm{\theta},\sigma;\epsilon)=\{\{\bm{x},v\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}:\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2},\lvert v-\sigma\rvert\leq\epsilon\}
. Then because
\sup{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\lvert\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h(\mu,\sigma)\rvert\leq c
and
\sup{\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h(\mu,\sigma)\rvert\leq c
for some constant
c>0
by Lemma
S8.25
, it is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta}_{1},\sigma_{1}\},\{\bm{\theta}_{2},\sigma_{2}\}\in B(\bm{\theta},\sigma;\epsilon)}\lvert\tilde{F}_{g}(\bm{\theta}_{1},\sigma_{1})-\tilde{F}_{g}(\bm{\theta}_{2},\sigma_{2})\rvert=O(\epsilon).

Therefore,
\tilde{F}_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
is stochastically equicontinuous on a compact set, which means
\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{S}}\allowbreak\lvert F_{g}(\bm{\theta})\rvert=o_{P}(1)
, and therefore implies
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}
are consistent.

Next, define

\displaystyle\bm{s}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\nabla f^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=(\bm{s}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)^{\top},s_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma))^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\nabla\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=(\tilde{\bm{s}}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)^{\top},\tilde{s}_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma))^{\top},

where
s_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma),\tilde{s}_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}
are partial derivatives with respect to
\sigma
. Then

\displaystyle\bm{s}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}/\sigma^{2}+(1-r_{gi})\alpha_{g}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{s}}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})-\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}/\sigma^{2}+(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i},\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}

\displaystyle s_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}^{2}/\sigma^{3}+(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})-\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}^{2}/\sigma^{3}+(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i},\sigma\}.

Since
\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}
is a bounded random variable and
\gamma=o(n^{-1/4})
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{\bm{s}}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-\bm{s}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})=&(n\sigma_{g}^{2})^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{Z}_{i*}\\
&+n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}),\sigma_{g}\}\bm{Z}_{i*}\\
&+n^{-1}\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}^{2}\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial^{3}\mu}h(\tilde{\mu}_{i},\sigma_{g})\bm{Z}_{i*},\end{aligned}

(S8.38)

where
\tilde{\mu}_{i}=\alpha_{i}\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g})+(1-\alpha_{i})\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}
for
\alpha_{i}\in[0,1]
. To show this difference is
o(n^{-1/2})
, we first note that because the terms inside each of the three summands in (
S8.38
) are independent with uniformly bounded moments and
\gamma=o(n^{-1/4})
, all three sums in (
S8.38
) have variance equal to
o(n^{-1/2})
. We therefore need only show that the expectation of the above three sums is
o(n^{-1/2})
. Since
\gamma^{2}=o(n^{-1/2})
and
\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial^{3}\mu}h(\tilde{\mu}_{i},\sigma_{g})
is uniformly bounded from above and below by Lemma
S8.25
, the expectation of the third sum is
o(n^{-1/2})
. Next, for the first sum in (
S8.38
),

\displaystyle\E\left(n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{Z}_{i*}\right)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{Z}_{i*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*})+o(n^{-1/2}),

where
\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*}
is independent of
\bm{G}_{s_{g}*}
by Assumption
S8.4
. Further,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\lvert\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}-\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})\rvert=o(n^{-1/4}),\quad\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})=\bm{X}_{i*}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{g}+\{\bm{\Delta}^{(c)}_{i*}+\textstyle\sum_{s\neq s_{g}}\bm{G}_{si}\bm{\gamma}^{(c)}_{s*}\}^{\top}\bm{\ell}_{g},

where
\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})
is independent of
\bm{G}_{s_{g}*}
by Assumption
S8.4
. Therefore, since
\frac{d}{dx}\Psi(x)
is bounded,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\lvert\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*})-\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*})\rvert

\displaystyle=o\left\{n^{-1/4}\sup_{i\in[n]}\E(\lVert\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*}\rVert_{2})\right\}=o(n^{-1/4})

Putting this all together gives us

\displaystyle\E\left(n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\bm{Z}_{i*}\right)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*})+o(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E(\bm{G}_{s_{g}i})\E(\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\bm{\Delta}^{(e)}_{gi}-\delta_{g}\}]\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i*})+o(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{-1/2}),

where the second equality follows because
\bm{G}_{s_{g}*}
is independent of
\{\tilde{\mu}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}),\bm{\Delta}^{(e)},\tilde{\bm{Z}}\}
and the third because
\E(\bm{G}_{s_{g}i})=0
. This implies the first sum in (
S8.38
) is
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. For the second and final term in (
S8.38
), we note that for
\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})
as defined above,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[n]}\lvert\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}),\sigma_{g}\}-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\mu}h\{\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}),\sigma_{g}\}\rvert=o(n^{-1/4})

by Assumption
S8.4
and Lemma
S8.25
. An identical analysis to the one applied to the first term of (
S8.38
) can then be used to show the second term of (
S8.38
) is
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.

We next consider the difference

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-s_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n\sigma_{g}^{3}}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})\}\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\theta),\sigma\}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial^{2}\mu}h(\tilde{\mu}_{i},\sigma)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\tilde{\mu}_{i}=\alpha_{i}\mu(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+(1-\alpha_{i})\{\mu(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}
for some
\alpha_{i}\in[0,1]
. Identical techniques to those used to show
\lVert\tilde{\bm{s}}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-\bm{s}_{1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
can be used to show
\tilde{s}_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-s_{2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. The details have been omitted. Putting all this together implies
\lVert\bm{s}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.

We next consider the Hessians:

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}\bm{s}^{\known}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[r_{gi}/\sigma^{2}-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}\bm{Z}_{i*}^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\known}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[r_{gi}/\sigma^{2}-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i},\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}\bm{Z}_{i*}^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}s^{\known}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[3r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}^{2}/\sigma^{4}-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{H}}_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\tilde{s}^{\known}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[3r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})-\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}^{2}/\sigma^{4}

\displaystyle-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i},\sigma\}]

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}s^{\known}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[2r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}/\sigma^{3}

\displaystyle-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{H}}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}\tilde{s}^{\known}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[2r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})-\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i}\}/\sigma^{3}

\displaystyle-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\gamma\bm{G}_{s_{g}i},\sigma\}]\bm{Z}_{i*}.

Let
B(\bm{\theta},\sigma;\epsilon)
be the Euclidean ball with radius
\epsilon
and centered at
\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}
defined above. Using Lemma
S8.25
, it is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}_{ij}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{ij}(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon),\quad i,j\in[2]

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\tilde{\bm{H}}_{ij}(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g})-\bm{H}_{ij}(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon),\quad i,j\in[2]

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{\bm{H}}_{ij}(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g})-\E\{\tilde{\bm{H}}_{ij}(\bm{\theta}_{g},\sigma_{g})\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}),\quad i,j\in[2].

Let
\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}
and
\tilde{\bm{H}}_{g}^{\known}
be the
(d+K+1)\times(d+K+1)
be the minus Hessians of
f^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
and
\tilde{f}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
evaluated at
(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})
. Note that the first
(d+K)\times(d+K)
and last diagonal elements of
\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}
and
\tilde{\bm{H}}_{g}^{\known}
are given by
\bm{H}_{11}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g}),\tilde{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})
and
H_{22}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g}),\tilde{H}_{22}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})
, and the off diagonal is given by
\bm{H}_{12}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g}),\tilde{\bm{H}}_{12}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})
. It is straightforward to show that
\E(\tilde{\bm{H}}_{g})\succeq cI_{d+K+1}
for some constant
c>0
and all
n
large enough. Putting all this together implies

\displaystyle\{n\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\}^{1/2}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\known}-\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}\}=\{\E(\tilde{\bm{H}}_{g})\}^{-1/2}\{n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\}+o_{P}(1).

The result then follows by an application of the Lindeberg central limit theorem.
∎

Theorem
S8.3
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
S8.2
and Lemma
S8.22
hold, let
\hat{\bm{C}}
,
\hat{\bm{Z}}
,
\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
,
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\ipw}
, and
\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\ipw}
be as defined in the statements of Lemma
S8.20
and
S8.21
, let
\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.22
, and define the log-likelihood function

\displaystyle f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[-r_{gi}\{\bm{y}_{gi}-\hat{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta})\}^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(1-r_{gi})\log(1-\smallint\phi(\epsilon)\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\hat{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\sigma\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\text{d}\epsilon)\right],\quad\hat{\mu}_{i}(\bm{\theta})=\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\bm{\theta},

where
\phi
is the probability density function of the standard normal. Let
m\geq 1
be a constant integer, and define
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\fs}=(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\fs}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\fs})^{\top}
to be the estimator for
\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}=(\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top},\sigma_{g})^{\top}
that, for starting point
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}=(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\ipw}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\ipw})^{\top}
, uses
J\in[m]
iterations of Fisher scoring to maximize
f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
. Then as
n\to\infty
and for
\hat{\bm{H}}_{g}^{\fs}
the plug-in estimator for
\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}
that plugs in
\hat{\bm{C}}
for
\bm{C}
and
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\fs}
for
\bm{\eta}_{g}^{*}
,

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\lvert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g_{j}}^{\fs}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g_{j}}^{\known}\rvert=o_{P}(1),\quad j\in[d_{1}]

(S8.39)

\displaystyle\lvert[\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\fs}\}^{-1}]_{rs}-[\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\}^{-1}]_{rs}\rvert=o_{P}(1),\quad r,s\in[d_{1}].

(S8.40)

Remark
S8.11
.

The first
d_{1}
entries of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\fs}
and
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}
are estimates for the first
d_{1}
entries of
\bm{\beta}_{g}
, which are exactly the coefficients of interest. Theorem
S8.3
therefore implies estimation for and inference on the coefficients of interest with our estimated
\bm{C}
is asymptotically equivalent to that when
\bm{C}
is known. A trivial corollary of Lemma
S8.22
and Theorem
S8.3
is that our Fisher scoring estimator for the coefficients of interest is asymptotically normal, where the first
d_{1}\times d_{1}
block of
\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\fs}\}^{-1}
is an estimator for its asymptotic variance.

Proof.

Let
\Theta,\mathcal{S}
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.22
and define

\displaystyle h(\mu,\sigma)=\log[\smallint\Psi\{-\alpha_{g}(\mu+\sigma e-\delta_{g})\}\phi(e)de].

We prove Theorem
S8.3
by showing that (a)
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}\}=\argmax_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
are asymptotically equivalent to
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}
defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.22
, and (b) that
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\fs},\sigma_{g}^{\fs}\}
are asymptotically equivalent to
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}\}
.

For (a), let
\bm{R}_{g}=\diag(r_{g1},\ldots,r_{gn})
and
\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1}=P_{\bm{X}_{2}}^{\perp}\bm{X}_{1}
. Then for
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta
and
\bar{\bm{\ell}}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
entries
d_{1}+1,\ldots,d_{1}+K
of
\bm{\theta}
,

\displaystyle f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-2}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y_{g}}^{\top})^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\Delta}_{C}\bar{\bm{\ell}}

\displaystyle+\sigma^{-2}\bar{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{C}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\{\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}+(n^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1})(\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}\}\bar{\bm{\ell}}

\displaystyle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\bar{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{C}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\Delta}_{C}\bar{\bm{\ell}}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-r_{gi})\bm{\delta}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\ell}}a_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{C}=

\displaystyle(\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}-\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v})+(n^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1})(\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}-\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{i}=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}}-\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}})+(\hat{\bm{\Omega}}_{1}-\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{1})\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1_{i\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}},\quad i\in[n]

\displaystyle a_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle-\alpha_{g}\frac{\smallint\dot{\Psi}[-\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\zeta_{gi}\bm{\delta}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\ell}}+\sigma_{g}\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\phi(\epsilon)d\epsilon}{\smallint\Psi[-\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\zeta_{gi}\bm{\delta}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\ell}}+\sigma\epsilon-\delta_{g}\}]\phi(\epsilon)d\epsilon},\quad\zeta_{gi}\in[0,1],\quad i\in[n],

where Theorems
S8.1
and
S8.2
imply
\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{C}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\lambda^{-1/2+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
, and Corollary
S8.5
and Theorem
S8.2
imply
\sup_{\bm{\theta}_{g}\in\Theta,i\in[n]}\lvert\bm{\delta}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
for some sufficiently small
\eta>0
. Since
\lvert a_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert\leq c
for some constant
c>0
by Lemma
S8.25
, this implies
\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-\eta})
for some sufficiently small
\eta>0
. Therefore, for
\tilde{f}^{\known}
as defined in Lemma
S8.22
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\E\{\tilde{f}_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\mid\bm{C},\bm{G}\}\rvert\leq\underbrace{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rvert}_{O_{P}(n^{-\eta})}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\E\{\tilde{f}_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\mid\bm{C},\bm{G}\}\rvert}_{\text{$o_{P}(1)$ by properties of \eqref{supp:equation:ftildefknown} in Lemma~\ref{supp:lemma:Cknown}}},

meaning
\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}-\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
and
\lvert\hat{\sigma}_{g}-\sigma_{g}\rvert=o_{P}(1)
. Next, define

\displaystyle\bm{Z}=[(n^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1}),\{\tilde{\bm{C}}\bm{v}+(n^{-1/2}\tilde{\bm{X}}_{1})(\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}\},(n^{-1/2}\bm{X}_{2})],\quad n^{1/2}\bm{Z}\bm{\theta}=(\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\ldots,\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}))^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{s}_{g1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\sigma^{-2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})+n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle s_{g2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\frac{\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{1}_{n}}{n\sigma}+\sigma^{-3}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{h}}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{11}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\bm{Z}^{\top}\{\sigma^{-2}\bm{R}_{g}-(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\}\bm{Z}

\displaystyle H_{22}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}f_{g}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=3\sigma^{-4}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})-\frac{\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{1}_{n}}{n\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\bm{1}_{n}

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{12}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}s_{g2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=2\sigma^{-3}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle-n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\bm{1}_{n}

\displaystyle\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\diag\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right]

\displaystyle\ddot{\bm{H}}_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\diag\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right]

\displaystyle\ddot{\bm{H}}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\diag\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right]

and

\displaystyle\bm{s}_{g1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\bm{s}_{g1}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)+\sigma^{-2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})+\sigma^{-2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle+n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)+n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle+n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle s_{g2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=s_{g2}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-2\sigma^{-3}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\bm{1}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\theta}}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma),\quad H_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma),\quad\bm{H}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}=

\displaystyle[\bm{0}_{n\times d_{1}},\bm{\Delta}_{C},\bm{0}_{n\times d_{2}}]

(S8.41)

\displaystyle\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta})+n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{1*}^{\top}\bm{\theta},\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta})+n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{n*}^{\top}\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\right)^{\top}-\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle\bm{\varepsilon}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta})+n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{1*}^{\top}\bm{\theta},\sigma\},\ldots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta})+n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{n*}^{\top}\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\right)^{\top}-\dot{\bm{h}}_{2}(\bm{\theta},\sigma),

where the
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
term is uniform over all
\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\Theta\times\mathcal{S}
. We prove two critical lemmas regarding the behavior
\bm{s}_{g1},s_{g2}
and
\bm{H}_{11},H_{22},\bm{H}_{12}
.

Lemma
S8.23
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
S8.3
hold and let
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
and
\tilde{\sigma}>0
be such that
\lVert\tilde{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2},\lvert\tilde{\sigma}-\sigma_{g}\rvert=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. Then

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{s}_{g1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})-\bm{s}_{g1}^{\known}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})\rVert_{2},\,\lVert s_{g2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})-s_{g2}^{\known}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

We prove the result for
\bm{s}_{g1}
. The proof for
s_{g2}
uses identical arguments, and has been omitted. The proof of Corollary
S8.6
can be used to show that

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}(n^{-1/2}\bm{y}_{g}-\bm{Z}\bm{\theta})\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{y}_{g}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{Z}\rVert_{2}\lVert\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2}\leq(1+\lVert\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2})o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{R}_{g}\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}\lVert\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\bm{\theta}\rVert_{2}o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

for any
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta
. Next, for any
\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\Theta\times\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\tilde{\sigma})=

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}^{\top}\bm{V}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\bm{\delta}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle\bm{V}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\diag\left[(1-r_{g1})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta}_{g})+\zeta_{1}(n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{1\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{g}),\sigma\},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\ldots,(1-r_{gn})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta})+\zeta_{n}(n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\displaystyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\textstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptstyle*$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right].

for some
\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n}\in[0,1]
that depend on
\bm{\theta}
and
\sigma
. Since
\lVert\bm{V}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rVert_{2}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
that does not depend on
\bm{\theta}
or
\sigma
by Lemma
S8.25
,
\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in\Theta\times\mathcal{S}}\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\tilde{\sigma})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
by Theorems
S8.1
and
S8.2
. Next, since the entries of
\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
have uniformly bounded gradient (and entries) by Lemma
S8.25
and
\lVert\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
,

\displaystyle\lVert n^{-1/2}\bm{\delta}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\sigma)\rVert_{2}=\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}_{1}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma)\rVert_{2}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

by Theorem
S8.1
for
\hat{\bm{z}}
and
\bm{Q}
as defined in (
S8.19
). Lemma
S8.2
, along with the same techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.6
, can be used to prove
\lVert n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{Q}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\dot{\bm{h}}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. The details have been omitted. Lastly,

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{\varepsilon}_{1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})=\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})\bm{\delta}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}+\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{r}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})

\displaystyle\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\frac{1}{2n^{1/2}}\left((n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{1*}^{\top}\bm{\theta})^{2}\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\mu^{3}}h\{\mu_{1}(\bm{\theta})+\zeta_{1},\sigma\},\ldots,(n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{n*}^{\top}\bm{\theta})^{2}\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\mu^{3}}h\{\mu_{n}(\bm{\theta})+\zeta_{n},\sigma\}\right)^{\top}

for
\zeta_{i}=\alpha_{i}n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{i*}^{\top}\bm{\theta}
and some
\alpha_{i}\in[0,1]
. Since the
d+1,\ldots,d+K
entries of
n^{1/2}\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}
are
O(\lambda^{1/2})
by Assumption
S8.4
, Corollary
S8.5
implies
\sup_{i\in[n]}(n^{1/2}\bm{\delta}_{i*}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\theta}})^{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. Therefore,
\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\bm{r}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{1/2})
by Lemma
S8.25
. Finally, since
\lVert\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})-\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
by Lemma
S8.25
and
\lVert\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\sigma})\bm{\delta}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}\{\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}\}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

An application of Lemma
S8.2
and identical techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.6
can be used to show
\lVert\bm{Z}^{\top}(I_{n}-\bm{R}_{g})\ddot{\bm{H}}_{11}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\bm{\delta}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.24
.

Suppose the assumptions in the statement of Theorem
S8.3
hold, let
\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}
be as defined in Lemma
S8.22
, and let
B(\bm{\theta},\sigma;\epsilon)
be the Euclidean ball centered at
(\bm{\theta}^{\top},\sigma)^{\top}
with radius
\epsilon>0
. Then for all
\epsilon
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon)+o_{P}(1)

(S8.42a)

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon)+o_{P}(1)

(S8.42b)

where
\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-\nabla^{2}f_{g}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
and
\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{F}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)&\bm{F}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\\
\bm{F}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)^{\top}&F_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\end{pmatrix}
is the Fisher information matrix given by

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{q\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}}{\sigma^{2}}-[1-q\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right)\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i*}\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i*}^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(2\sigma^{-2}\smallint e\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\sigma e-\delta_{g}\}]\phi(e)de\right.

\displaystyle\left.-[1-q\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\mu\partial\sigma}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right)\hat{\bm{Z}}_{i*}

\displaystyle F_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sigma^{-2}\smallint(3e^{2}-1)\Psi[\alpha_{g}\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta})+\sigma e-\delta_{g}\}]\phi(e)de\right.

\displaystyle\left.-[1-q\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}]\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}h\{\mu_{i}(\bm{\theta}),\sigma\}\right)

\displaystyle q(\mu,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\smallint\Psi\{\alpha_{g}(\mu+\sigma e-\delta_{g})\}\phi(e)de

Remark
S8.12
.

Result (
S8.40
) in the statement of Theorem
S8.3
follows from (
S8.39
) and (
S8.42a
). We therefore need only prove (
S8.39
) to complete the proof of Theorem
S8.3
.

Remark
S8.13
.

Since
\lVert\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}-\E\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\mid\bm{G},\bm{C}\}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
, Lemma
S8.24
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\E\{\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\mid\bm{G},\bm{C}\}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon)+o_{P}(1).

Proof.

We first note that

\displaystyle\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{H}_{11}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)&\bm{H}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\\
\bm{H}_{12}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)^{\top}&H_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}=\bm{H}^{\known}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{H}_{11}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)&\bm{H}_{12}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\\
\bm{H}_{12}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)^{\top}&H^{\known}_{22}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rVert_{2},

where the first term after the
\leq
is
O_{P}(\epsilon)+o_{P}(1)
by the proof of Lemma
S8.22
. Straightforward applications of Theorem
S8.1
and Lemma
S8.25
can be used to show

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{H}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1),

which proves (
S8.42a
). For (
S8.42b
), we see that

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{F}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}.

The same techniques used to prove
\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
. Lastly, An application of Lemma
S8.25
can be used to prove

\displaystyle\sup_{\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}\in B(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g};\epsilon)}\lVert\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)-\bm{F}(\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*},\sigma_{g})\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(\epsilon)+o_{P}(1),

which completes the proof.
∎

Returning to the proof of Theorem
S8.3
, the observation that
(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})^{\top}
and
(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known})^{\top}
are consistent for
(\{\bm{\theta}_{g}^{*}\}^{\top},\sigma_{g})^{\top}
, as well as Lemma
S8.24
imply

\displaystyle 0=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\\
s_{g2}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\\
s_{g2}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}^{\known}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\\
s_{g2}^{\known}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}^{\known}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\\
s_{g2}^{\known}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\end{pmatrix}}_{0}-\bm{H}_{g}^{\known}\bm{\Delta}+o_{P}(\lVert\bm{\Delta}\rVert_{2})

for
\bm{\Delta}=(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g})^{\top}-(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known})^{\top}
. Since
\lVert(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known})^{\top}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
, Lemma
S8.23
implies
\lVert\bm{\Delta}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
, which completes part (a) (the first part of the proof; see above).

For part (b) (the second part of the proof; see above), let
\bm{F}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+K+1)\times(d+K+1)}
be the Fisher scoring matrix defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.24
. Then for
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j)}=(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}_{g}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{(j)})^{\top}
the
j
th Fisher scoring updates,
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}=(\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{\ipw}_{g}\}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\ipw})^{\top}
, and
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}=(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\top},\hat{\sigma}_{g})^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(1)}=\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}+[\bm{F}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}\}]^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}\}\\
s_{g2}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}\}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j+1)}=\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j)}+[\bm{F}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j)}\}]^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j)}\}\\
s_{g2}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(j)}\}\end{pmatrix},\quad j=1,\ldots,m-1.

We study the behavior of
\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(1)}
for simplicity, and note the extension to finite
j>1
is trivial. Since
\lVert\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}-\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{*}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
by Lemmas
S8.20
and
S8.21
,
\lVert\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}-\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}\rVert_{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. Lemma
S8.24
then implies

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}+[\bm{F}\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}\}]^{-1}\left[\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\bm{s}_{g1}(\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g})\\
s_{g2}(\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g})\end{pmatrix}}_{0}-\smallint_{0}^{1}\bm{H}\{t\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}+(1-t)\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}\}dt\{\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}^{\ipw}-\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\eta}}_{g}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
S8.25
.

Let
c,m,M>0
be constants and suppose
\Psi(x)
is a six times continuously differentiable cumulative distribution function, where

(i)

\Psi(-x)=1-\Psi(x)
and
\lvert\Psi^{(j)}(x)\rvert\leq c
for all
j\in[6]
.

(ii)

\lvert x\rvert^{m}\Psi(x)\geq c
for all
x<-M

(iii)

\lvert x\rvert^{m}\lvert\Psi^{(j)}(x)\rvert\leq c
for
j\in[6]
and all
\lvert x\rvert>M
.

Define
\mu(x,\sigma)=\log\{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)\text{d}e\}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma\in(s^{-1},s)
for some constant
s>1
. Then for some constant
\tilde{c}
and
i,j\geq 0
such that
i+j\in[3]
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{\partial^{(i+j)}}{\partial x^{i}\partial\sigma^{j}}\mu(x,\sigma)\right\rvert\leq\tilde{c}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\frac{\smallint\dot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\frac{\smallint\ddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\left\{\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\frac{\smallint\dddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\frac{\smallint\ddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}\frac{\smallint\dot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle-2\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma\frac{\smallint\ddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(4)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\left\{\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\right\}^{2}+\frac{\smallint\ddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma^{3}\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(6)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\sigma^{3}\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(4)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}\frac{\smallint\ddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle-2\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)+3\sigma\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(4)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\sigma^{-1}\left\{\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{1}\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma\frac{\smallint\dddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)
