\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{2}\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(4)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\sigma\frac{\smallint\dddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)-\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{1}\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{1}\partial\sigma^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(5)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}-\frac{\smallint\dddot{\Psi}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}\left\{\sigma\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)-1\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial x^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\mu(x,\sigma)-\frac{\partial^{1}}{\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{1}\partial\sigma^{1}}\mu(x,\sigma).

Since
\sigma
is bounded above 0 and below
\infty
, we therefore only have to show that
\left\lvert\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}\right\rvert
is bounded from above for all
j\in[6]
to prove the lemma. First,
\lvert\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\rvert
is bounded from above. Second,
\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de>0
is increasing in
\sigma
for all
\lvert x\rvert
suitably large. Third,
\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de
is increasing in
x
for all fixed
\sigma
. The latter two imply
\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de>a_{k}
for all
x>-k
and
\sigma\in(s^{-1},s)
, where
k>0
and
a_{k}>0
is a constant that only depends on
k
. These three imply we need only consider the behavior of
\frac{\smallint\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de}
when
(-x)
is large to prove the lemma. Let
M
,
c
, and
m
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.25
. Then for
Z\sim N(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de\geq

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\Psi(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de\geq c\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\lvert x+\sigma e\rvert^{-m}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\{(-x+\sigma Z)^{-m}1\{-x+\sigma Z\geq M\}\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle[\E\{(-x+\sigma Z)1\{-x+\sigma Z\geq M\}\}]^{-m}\geq(-x/2)^{-m},

where the last inequality holds for all
(-x)>0
sufficiently large. Further, for all
(-x)>0
sufficiently large and some constant
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de=&\int_{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}^{\frac{M-x}{\sigma}}\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de+\int_{\frac{M-x}{\sigma}}^{\infty}\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de\\
&+\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\Psi^{(j)}(x+\sigma e)\phi(e)de\\
\leq&\frac{2cM}{\sigma}\phi\left(\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}\right)+\epsilon\frac{\phi\left(\frac{M-x}{\sigma}\right)}{(M-x)/\sigma}\\
&+c\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\lvert x+\sigma e\rvert^{-m}\phi(e)de.\end{aligned}

(S8.43)

First,
\lvert x\rvert^{m}\phi\left(\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}\right)
is bounded from above as a function of
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma\in(s^{-1},s)
. Second,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\lvert x+\sigma e\rvert^{-m}\phi(e)de=&\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{-M-x}{2\sigma}}\lvert x+\sigma e\rvert^{-m}\phi(e)de+\int_{\frac{-M-x}{2\sigma}}^{\frac{-M-x}{\sigma}}\lvert x+\sigma e\rvert^{-m}\phi(e)de\\
\leq&\left\lvert\frac{(-x)+M}{2}\right\rvert^{-m}+M^{-m}\phi\left\{\frac{(-x)-M}{2\sigma}\right\}\end{aligned}

(S8.44)

for all
(-x)>0
sufficiently large, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark
S8.14
.

If we replace condition (iii) in the statement of Lemma
S8.25
with
\lvert x\rvert^{m+\delta}\allowbreak\lvert\Psi^{(j)}(x)\rvert\allowbreak\leq c
for any
\delta>0
, we would replace
-m
with
-(m+\delta)
in (
S8.43
) and (
S8.44
), which would prove that
\left\lvert\frac{\partial^{i+j}}{\partial x^{i}\partial\sigma^{j}}\mu(x,\sigma)\right\rvert\to 0
as
\lvert x\rvert\to\infty
. This shows that outlying missing data points have a trivial contribution to the gradient of the log-likelihood in (
4.4
), suggesting that letting
\Psi
be the CDF of a t-distribution makes estimation robust to outliers.

S9
Theoretical guarantees for mtGWAS

S9.1
A restatement of Theorem
5.4

Before proving our results for mtGWAS, we first redefine
\eta_{gs}^{(e)}
,
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}
, and
\eta_{gs}^{(c,e)}
to all observed nuisance covariates
\bm{x}_{i}
. First,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \eta_{gs}^{(e)}=&\{\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}(\gamma,\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\mid_{\gamma=0}\}^{2}[\{-\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\}^{-1}]_{11}\\
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}\}=&\argmax_{\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K},\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{gsi}(0,\bm{\theta},\sigma)\\
h_{gsi}(\gamma,\bm{\theta},\sigma)=&-r_{gi}\{y_{gi}-(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\gamma G_{si})\}^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})\\
&+(1-r_{gi})\log[1-\smallint\Psi\{\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\gamma G_{si}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)\text{d}e]\\
\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}=&(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\hat{\bm{c}}_{i})^{\top},\end{aligned}

(S9.1)

where we solve the optimization problem in the second line using the one-step Fisher scoring algorithm detailed in the statement of Theorem
5.3
. The matrix
\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)
is the standard
(K+d+1)\times(K+d+1)
Fisher information matrix evaluated at
\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}
and using covariates
\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}
. We next define
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}
to be

\displaystyle\eta_{gs}^{(c)}=\frac{\{\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}^{2}}{\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\top}\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}+\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}^{\top}\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g})\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}},\quad\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}=(\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}_{s})^{-1}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\hat{\bm{C}},

(S9.2)

where
\bm{G}_{s}=(G_{s1},\ldots,G_{sn})^{\top}
. The estimate
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
is the appropriate sub-vector of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}
defined in (
S9.1
),
\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g})
is the appropriate
K\times K
sub-matrix
\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})
defined in (
S9.1
), and
\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}
is the usual ordinary least squares estimate for the variance of
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}
from the regression of
\hat{\bm{C}}
onto
\bm{G}_{s}
and
\bm{X}
. We can now re-state Theorem
5.4
.

Theorem
S9.4
.

Suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, fix a
g\in[p]
, and let
\eta_{gs}^{(e)}
and
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}
be as defined in (
S9.1
) and (
S9.2
). Then
\eta_{gs}^{(e)}\tdist\chi^{2}_{1}
if
H_{0,gs}^{(e)}:\gamma_{gs}^{(e)}=0
is true. If (i)
n^{1/2}\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2}\to\infty
and (ii)
\E(\bm{c}_{i}\mid G_{si})=\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{x}_{i}+\bm{\gamma}_{s}^{(c)}G_{si}
for some non-random
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K}
, then
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}\tdist\chi^{2}_{1}
if
H_{0,gs}^{(c)}:\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}\gamma_{s}^{(c)}=0
is true and
\eta_{gs}^{(c,e)}=\eta_{gs}^{(c)}+\eta_{gs}^{(e)}\tdist\chi^{2}_{2}
if
H_{0,gs}^{(c,e)}:\bm{\ell}_{g}^{\top}\gamma_{s}^{(c)}=\gamma_{gs}^{(e)}=0
is true.

S9.2
Proof of Theorem
S9.4

We prove Theorem
S9.4
by first showing that
\eta_{gs}^{(e)}
and
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}
are asymptotically equivalent to the corresponding quantities when
\bm{C}
is known and when we account for all genetic effects on
e_{gi}
.

Lemma
S9.26
.

Fix a
g\in[p]
and
s\in[S]
, suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, and let
\bm{z}_{i}=(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\bm{c}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
. Define
\mathcal{H}_{g}=\{r\in[S]:\gamma^{(e)}_{gr}\neq 0\}
and
a_{gs},\bm{A}_{gs},a_{gs}^{\known},\bm{A}_{gs}^{\known}
to be

\displaystyle a_{gs}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}(\gamma,\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\mid_{\gamma=0},\quad a_{gs}^{\known}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}^{\known}\{\gamma,\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}\mid_{\gamma=0}

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{gs}=n^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}),\quad n^{-1}\bm{A}_{gs}^{\known}=\mathcal{I}_{gs}^{\known}\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}

\displaystyle h_{gsi}^{\known}(\gamma,\theta,\sigma)=-r_{gi}\log(\sigma)-r_{gi}\left[y_{gi}-\left\{\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}+G_{si}\gamma+\sum_{r\in\mathcal{H}_{g}\setminus\{s\}}\gamma^{(e)}_{ri}G_{ri}\right\}\right]^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle+(1-r_{gi})\log\left(\int\Psi\left[\alpha_{g}\left\{\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}+G_{si}\gamma+\sum_{r\in\mathcal{H}_{g}\setminus\{s\}}\gamma^{(e)}_{ri}G_{ri}+\sigma e\right\}\right]\phi(e)\text{d}e\right)

\displaystyle\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}^{\known}\}=\argmax_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{gsi}(0,\bm{\theta},\sigma).

where
h_{gsi}
,
\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}\}
, and
\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})
are defined in (
S9.1
),
\Theta,\mathcal{S}
are as defined in the statement of Lemma
S8.22
, and
\mathcal{I}_{gs}^{\known}\{\bm{\theta},\sigma\}
is the corresponding
(d+K+1)\times(d+K+1)
minus Fisher information matrix evaluated at
\{\gamma=0,\bm{\theta},\sigma\}
. Then if the null hypothesis
H_{0,gs}^{(e)}:\gamma_{gs}^{(e)}=0
is true, then
n^{1/2}\lvert a_{gs}-a_{gs}^{\known}\rvert=o_{P}(1)
and
\lVert\bm{A}_{gs}-\bm{A}_{gs}^{\known}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
.

Proof.

Note that
\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}^{\known}(\gamma,\bm{\theta},\sigma)\mid_{\gamma=0}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}(\gamma,\bm{\theta},\sigma)\mid_{\gamma=0}
are exactly the score functions from Lemma
S8.22
and Theorem
S8.3
. Therefore, the results are a simple consequence of the proofs and results of Lemma
S8.22
and Theorem
S8.3
.
∎

Lemma
S9.27
.

Fix an
s\in[S]
, suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, and let
\bm{b}_{gs}
,
\bm{B}_{gs}
,
\bm{b}_{gs}^{\known}
, and
\bm{B}_{gs}^{\known}
be

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}=\{(\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}_{s})^{-1}\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(n^{1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp})\}^{\top},\quad\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}=\{(\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}\bm{G}_{s})^{-1}\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}P_{\bm{X}}^{\perp}(n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}=n^{-1}(n^{1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp})^{\top}P_{[\bm{X},\bm{G}_{s}]}^{\perp}(n^{1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}),\quad\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}\}=n^{-1}(n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}})^{\top}P_{[\bm{X},\bm{G}_{s}]}^{\perp}(n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}})

for
\bm{G}_{s}=(G_{s1},\ldots,G_{sn})^{\top}
and
\tilde{\bm{C}}
defined in (
S8.2
). Then for unitary matrix
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
as defined in the statement of Theorem
S8.1
,
n^{1/2}\lVert\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
and
\lVert\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}\}\bm{v}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
.

Proof.

The vector
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
is exactly the first column of

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s}&n^{-1/2}\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{X}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s}&n^{-1}\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}\bm{X}\\
n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{G}_{s}&n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}.

Therefore, to prove
n^{1/2}\lVert\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
, we need only show that
\lVert\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{G}_{s})-\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{G}_{s})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
and
\lVert\hat{\bm{C}}_{\perp}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})-\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{C}}^{\top}(n^{-1/2}\bm{X})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. However, this can easily be shown using the exact same techniques used to prove Corollary
S8.6
. The same goes for showing that
\lVert\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}\}-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}\}\bm{v}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
. The details have been omitted.
∎

Lemma
S9.28
.

Fix a
g\in[p]
, suppose Assumption
S8.4
holds, and let
\mathcal{H}_{g}=\{s\in[S]:\gamma^{(e)}_{gs}\neq 0\}
. Let
\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}=(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\hat{\bm{c}}_{i})^{\top}
and
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{i}=(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},n^{1/2}\tilde{\bm{C}}_{i*}^{\top})
for
\tilde{\bm{C}}
given in (
S8.2
). Let
\Theta
and
\mathcal{S}
be as given in the statement of Lemma
S8.22
, and define
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}
and
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}
to be

\displaystyle\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}\}=\argmax_{\bm{\theta},\sigma}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma),\quad\{\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known},\hat{\sigma}_{g}\}=\argmax_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta,\sigma\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{f}_{gi}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)

\displaystyle f_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-r_{gi}\log(\sigma)-r_{gi}\left(y_{gi}-\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}\right)^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})+(1-r_{gi})\log[\smallint\Psi\{\alpha_{g}(\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}+\sigma e)\}\phi(e)\text{d}e]

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{gi}^{\known}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=-r_{gi}\log(\sigma)-r_{gi}\left[y_{gi}-\left\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{H}_{g}}\gamma^{(e)}_{si}G_{si}\right\}\right]^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle+(1-r_{gi})\log\left(\int\Psi\left[\alpha_{g}\left\{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{H}_{g}}\gamma^{(e)}_{si}G_{si}+\sigma e\right\}\right]\phi(e)\text{d}e\right),

where the first optimization is solved using the one step Fisher scoring method outlined in the statement of Theorem
5.3
. Define
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
and
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}
to be the last
K
elements of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}
and
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g}^{\known}
, and let
\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g})
and
\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}\}
be the standard minus Fisher information-derived estimates for the variances. Then for
\bm{v}
given in the statement of Theorem
S8.1
,
n^{1/2}\lVert\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}\rVert=o_{P}(1)
and
n\lVert\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g})-\bm{v}^{\top}\hat{\V}\{\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}\}\bm{v}\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1)
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the proofs of Lemma
S8.22
and Theorem
S8.3
.
∎

We use these three lemmas to prove Theorem
S9.4
.

Proof of Theorem
S9.4
.

We first prove the properties of
\eta_{gs}^{(e)}
. If
H_{0,gs}^{(e)}
is true, Lemma
S9.26
implies it suffices to study the properties of
\eta_{gs}^{(e),\known}=\{a_{gs}^{\known}\}^{2}/[\{\bm{A}_{gs}^{\known}\}^{-1}]_{11}
. However, standard techniques can be used to show that this satisfies
\eta_{gs}^{(e),\known}\tdist\chi^{2}_{1}
.

We next consider
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}
. Let
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)},\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}
and
\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g},\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}
be as defined in Lemmas
S9.27
and
S9.28
. To simplify notation, let
\hat{\bm{\ell}}=\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
,
\bar{\bm{\ell}}=\hat{\bm{\ell}}_{g}^{\known}
,
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}=\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c)}
, and
\bar{\bm{\gamma}}=\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{s}^{(c),\known}
. First, since
\bar{\bm{\ell}}_{g}
is estimated conditional on
\bm{C}
, it is straightforward to show that for
\tilde{\bm{\ell}}
as defined in (
S8.2
),

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\bm{\ell}}-\tilde{\bm{\ell}}\\
\bar{\bm{\gamma}}-\bm{\gamma}_{s}^{(c)}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{W}_{\ell}\\
\bm{W}_{\gamma}\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1),

where
\bm{W}_{\ell}\sim N_{K}(0,n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\ell}}))
and
\bm{W}_{\gamma}\sim N_{K}(0,n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\gamma}}))
are independent. Since
\Prob\{n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\ell}})\succeq cI_{K}\}
and
\Prob\{n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\gamma}})\succeq cI_{K}\}
go to 1 as
n\to\infty
for some constant
c>0
small enough,

\displaystyle b_{gs}=\frac{n^{1/2}\bar{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\gamma}}}{\{n\bar{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\gamma}})\bar{\bm{\ell}}+n\bar{\bm{\gamma}}^{\top}\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\ell}})\bar{\bm{\gamma}}\}^{1/2}}\tdist N(0,1)

when
H_{0,gs}^{(c)}
is true by the assumption that
n^{1/2}\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2}\to\infty
. Next, Lemmas
S9.27
and
S9.28
imply

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\lvert\hat{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}-\bar{\bm{\ell}}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\gamma}}\rvert=o_{P}(\lVert\bm{\gamma}_{s}^{(c)}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2}+n^{-1/2})=o_{P}(\lVert\bm{\gamma}_{s}^{(c)}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2})

\displaystyle\lVert n\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\gamma}})-n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\gamma}})\rVert_{2},\,\lVert n\hat{\V}(\hat{\bm{\ell}})-n\hat{\V}(\bar{\bm{\ell}})\rVert_{2}=o_{P}(1),

where the second equality in the first line follows from the fact that
n^{1/2}\lVert\bm{\ell}_{g}\rVert_{2}\to\infty
. This proves
\eta_{gs}^{(c)}\tdist\chi^{2}_{1}
when
H_{0,gs}^{(c)}
is true.

We lastly prove
\eta_{gs}^{(c,e)}=\eta_{gs}^{(c)}+\eta_{gs}^{(e)}\tdist\chi^{2}_{2}
when
H_{0,gs}^{(c,e)}
is true, which by Lemmas
S9.26
,
S9.27
, and
S9.28
, holds if
\eta_{gs}^{(e),\known}
is asymptotically independent of
\eta_{gs}^{(c),\known}=b_{gs}^{2}
under
H_{0,gs}^{(c,e)}
. Asymptotic independence holds because
a_{gs}^{\known}
,
\bar{\bm{\ell}}
, and
\bar{\bm{\gamma}}
are asymptotically independent, which completes the proof.
∎

S9.3
The computational efficiency of mtGWAS test statistics

The test statistic
\eta_{sg}^{(c)}
involves simply regressing estimated latent factors onto genotype via ordinary least squares, and is therefore easy to compute at the genome-wide scale. For
\eta_{sg}^{(e)}
, the partial derivative in (
S9.1
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}h_{gsi}(\gamma,\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\mid_{\gamma=0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{si}s_{gi}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})

\displaystyle s_{gi}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=r_{gi}(y_{gi}-\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i})/\sigma^{2}-(1-r_{gi})\frac{\hat{\alpha}_{g}\smallint\dot{\Psi}\{\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)\text{d}e}{1-\smallint\Psi\{\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)\text{d}e}

for
\dot{\Psi}(x)=\frac{d}{dx}\Psi(x)
. Since
s_{gi}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})
does not depend on genotype, it can pre-computed. For the minus inverse Fisher information, let
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}=\diag\{d_{g1}^{(11)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}),\ldots,d_{gn}^{(11)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\}
,
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}=\diag\{d_{g1}^{(12)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}),\ldots,d_{gn}^{(12)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\}
, and
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(22)}=\diag\{d_{g1}^{(22)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g}),\ldots,d_{gn}^{(22)}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\}
, where

\displaystyle d_{gi}^{(11)}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle\sigma^{-2}\smallint\Psi\{\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de-\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{2}\smallint\ddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle+\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{2}\frac{[\smallint\dot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de]^{2}}{\smallint\Psi\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle d_{gi}^{(12)}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{-2}\hat{\alpha}_{g}\smallint\dot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle+\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{3}\sigma\smallint\dddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle-\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{3}\sigma\frac{\smallint\dot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de\smallint\ddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de}{\smallint\Psi\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle d_{gi}^{(22)}(\bm{\theta},\sigma)=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{-2}\smallint\Psi\{\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle-4\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{2}\smallint\ddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

\displaystyle+\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{4}\sigma^{2}\frac{[\smallint\ddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de]^{2}}{\smallint\Psi\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de}

\displaystyle-\hat{\alpha}_{g}^{4}\sigma^{2}\smallint\ddddot{\Psi}\{-\hat{\alpha}_{g}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\hat{\bm{z}}_{i}+\sigma e-\hat{\delta}_{g})\}\phi(e)de

for
\ddot{\Psi}(x)
,
\dddot{\Psi}(x)
, and
\ddddot{\Psi}(x)
the second, third, and fourth derivatives of
\Psi
. Then
[\{-\mathcal{I}_{gs}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{g},\hat{\sigma}_{g})\}^{-1}]_{11}
is exactly the first diagonal element of

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}\bm{G}_{s}&\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}\hat{\bm{Z}}&\bm{G}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}\bm{1}_{n}\\
\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}\bm{G}_{s}&\hat{\bm{Z}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}\hat{\bm{Z}}&\hat{\bm{Z}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}\bm{1}_{n}\\
\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}\bm{G}_{s}&\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}\hat{\bm{Z}}&\bm{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(22)}\bm{1}_{n}\end{pmatrix}^{-1},

where
\bm{G}_{s}=(G_{s1},\ldots,G_{sn})^{\top}
and
\hat{\bm{Z}}=(\hat{\bm{z}}_{1}\cdots\hat{\bm{z}}_{n})^{\top}
. Since
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(11)}
,
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(12)}
, and
\hat{\bm{D}}_{g}^{(22)}
do not depend on genotype, they can be pre-computed.
